Idempotent Operations
Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Step 1: Create Idempotency Service
@Service
public class IdempotencyService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    private final Logger logger = LoggerFactory.getLogger(IdempotencyService.class);
    
    public IdempotencyService(RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate, 
                             ObjectMapper objectMapper) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.objectMapper = objectMapper;
    }
    
    public <T> Optional<T> executeWithIdempotency(String key, 
                                                  Supplier<T> operation, 
                                                  Duration ttl) {
        // Check if already processed
        String cachedResult = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(key);
        if (cachedResult != null) {
            logger.info("Idempotent request {} already processed", key);
            return Optional.of(deserializeResult(cachedResult));
        }
        
        // Try to acquire lock
        Boolean lockAcquired = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
            .setIfAbsent("lock:" + key, "locked", Duration.ofSeconds(30));
        
        if (Boolean.FALSE.equals(lockAcquired)) {
            logger.warn("Could not acquire lock for {}", key);
            return Optional.empty();
        }
        
        try {
            // Execute operation
            T result = operation.get();
            
            // Cache result
            String serialized = serializeResult(result);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(key, serialized, ttl);
            
            return Optional.of(result);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            logger.error("Operation failed for idempotent request {}", key, e);
            throw e;
        } finally {
            // Release lock
            redisTemplate.delete("lock:" + key);
        }
    }
    
    @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
    private <T> T deserializeResult(String cachedResult) {
        try {
            return (T) objectMapper.readValue(cachedResult, Object.class);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to deserialize cached result", e);
        }
    }
    
    private <T> String serializeResult(T result) {
        try {
            return objectMapper.writeValueAsString(result);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to serialize result", e);
        }
    }
}

// Step 2: Use in Controller
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    private final OrderService orderService;
    private final IdempotencyService idempotencyService;
    
    @PostMapping
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> createOrder(
            @Valid @RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request,
            @RequestHeader("Idempotency-Key") String idempotencyKey) {
        
        if (idempotencyKey == null || idempotencyKey.isEmpty()) {
            return ResponseEntity.badRequest().build();
        }
        
        Optional<OrderResponse> cachedResult = idempotencyService.executeWithIdempotency(
            idempotencyKey,
            () -> orderService.createOrder(request),
            Duration.ofHours(24)
        );
        
        if (cachedResult.isPresent()) {
            return ResponseEntity.ok(cachedResult.get());
        }
        
        // Return 202 Accepted for async processing
        return ResponseEntity.accepted()
            .header("X-Idempotency-Key", idempotencyKey)
            .build();
    }
}
2. Resilience & Fault Tolerance
Circuit Breaker Pattern (Resilience4j)
Step-by-Step Implementation:

Step 1: Add Dependencies

xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-aop</artifactId>
</dependency>
<dependency>
    <groupId>io.github.resilience4j</groupId>
    <artifactId>resilience4j-spring-boot2</artifactId>
    <version>2.1.0</version>
</dependency>
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-actuator</artifactId>
</dependency>
Step 2: Configure Circuit Breaker in application.yml

yaml
resilience4j:
  circuitbreaker:
    instances:
      payment-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 10
        minimum-number-of-calls: 5
        permitted-number-of-calls-in-half-open-state: 3
        automatic-transition-from-open-to-half-open-enabled: true
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 30s
        failure-rate-threshold: 50
        event-consumer-buffer-size: 10
        record-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException
          - java.io.IOException
      inventory-service:
        register-health-indicator: true
        sliding-window-size: 10
        failure-rate-threshold: 40
        wait-duration-in-open-state: 20s
    
  retry:
    instances:
      payment-service:
        max-attempts: 3
        wait-duration: 1s
        retry-exceptions:
          - org.springframework.web.client.HttpServerErrorException
          - java.io.IOException
Step 3: Implement Circuit Breaker with Annotations

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class PaymentServiceClient {
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate;
    private final CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry;
    
    @Autowired
    public PaymentServiceClient(RestTemplate restTemplate,
                               CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry) {
        this.restTemplate = restTemplate;
        this.circuitBreakerRegistry = circuitBreakerRegistry;
    }
    
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "payment-service", fallbackMethod = "processPaymentFallback")
    @Retry(name = "payment-service")
    public PaymentResponse processPayment(PaymentRequest request) {
        log.info("Processing payment for order: {}", request.getOrderId());
        
        ResponseEntity<PaymentResponse> response = restTemplate.postForEntity(
            "http://payment-service/api/payments",
            request,
            PaymentResponse.class
        );
        
        return response.getBody();
    }
    
    // Fallback method
    public PaymentResponse processPaymentFallback(PaymentRequest request, Exception ex) {
        log.warn("Payment service fallback triggered for order: {}", request.getOrderId(), ex);
        
        // Return fallback response
        return PaymentResponse.builder()
            .status(PaymentStatus.PENDING)
            .message("Payment processing is temporarily unavailable. Order will be processed later.")
            .orderId(request.getOrderId())
            .build();
    }
}

// Step 4: Custom Circuit Breaker Configuration
@Configuration
public class Resilience4jConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry() {
        CircuitBreakerConfig config = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            .slidingWindowType(CircuitBreakerConfig.SlidingWindowType.COUNT_BASED)
            .slidingWindowSize(10)
            .minimumNumberOfCalls(5)
            .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(3)
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(30))
            .failureRateThreshold(50)
            .recordException(throwable -> {
                // Record specific exceptions as failures
                return throwable instanceof HttpServerErrorException ||
                       throwable instanceof TimeoutException;
            })
            .build();
        
        return CircuitBreakerRegistry.of(config);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public TimedAspect timedAspect(MeterRegistry registry) {
        return new TimedAspect(registry);
    }
}
Retry with Exponential Backoff
Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Component
@Slf4j
public class RetryableService {
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate;
    
    @Retryable(
        value = {HttpServerErrorException.class, TimeoutException.class},
        maxAttempts = 5,
        backoff = @Backoff(delay = 1000, multiplier = 2, maxDelay = 60000)
    )
    public OrderDetails getOrderDetails(Long orderId) {
        log.info("Attempting to fetch order details for ID: {}", orderId);
        
        ResponseEntity<OrderDetails> response = restTemplate.getForEntity(
            "http://order-service/api/orders/{id}",
            OrderDetails.class,
            orderId
        );
        
        return response.getBody();
    }
    
    @Recover
    public OrderDetails recover(HttpServerErrorException e, Long orderId) {
        log.error("All retry attempts failed for order ID: {}", orderId, e);
        
        // Return cached or default response
        return OrderDetails.builder()
            .orderId(orderId)
            .status(OrderStatus.UNKNOWN)
            .error("Service temporarily unavailable")
            .build();
    }
}

// Custom Retry Configuration
@Configuration
public class RetryConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public RetryTemplate retryTemplate() {
        RetryTemplate retryTemplate = new RetryTemplate();
        
        // Exponential backoff policy
        ExponentialBackOffPolicy backOffPolicy = new ExponentialBackOffPolicy();
        backOffPolicy.setInitialInterval(1000); // 1 second
        backOffPolicy.setMultiplier(2.0);       // Double each time
        backOffPolicy.setMaxInterval(60000);    // Max 60 seconds
        
        retryTemplate.setBackOffPolicy(backOffPolicy);
        
        // Retry on specific exceptions
        SimpleRetryPolicy retryPolicy = new SimpleRetryPolicy();
        retryPolicy.setMaxAttempts(5);
        
        Map<Class<? extends Throwable>, Boolean> retryableExceptions = new HashMap<>();
        retryableExceptions.put(HttpServerErrorException.class, true);
        retryableExceptions.put(TimeoutException.class, true);
        retryableExceptions.put(ResourceAccessException.class, true);
        
        retryPolicy.setRetryableExceptions(retryableExceptions);
        retryTemplate.setRetryPolicy(retryPolicy);
        
        // Add retry listener
        retryTemplate.registerListener(new RetryListener() {
            @Override
            public <T, E extends Throwable> boolean open(RetryContext context, 
                                                         RetryCallback<T, E> callback) {
                log.info("Starting retry for method: {}", context.getAttribute("method"));
                return true;
            }
            
            @Override
            public <T, E extends Throwable> void onError(RetryContext context,
                                                         RetryCallback<T, E> callback,
                                                         Throwable throwable) {
                log.warn("Retry attempt {}/{} failed", 
                    context.getRetryCount(), 
                    context.getAttribute("maxAttempts"),
                    throwable);
            }
            
            @Override
            public <T, E extends Throwable> void close(RetryContext context,
                                                       RetryCallback<T, E> callback,
                                                       Throwable throwable) {
                if (throwable != null) {
                    log.error("All retry attempts exhausted", throwable);
                } else {
                    log.info("Operation succeeded after {} attempts", context.getRetryCount());
                }
            }
        });
        
        return retryTemplate;
    }
}
Bulkhead Isolation
Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Step 1: Configure Bulkhead in application.yml
resilience4j:
  bulkhead:
    instances:
      premium-tier-bulkhead:
        max-concurrent-calls: 50
        max-wait-duration: 100ms
      standard-tier-bulkhead:
        max-concurrent-calls: 20
        max-wait-duration: 50ms
      free-tier-bulkhead:
        max-concurrent-calls: 5
        max-wait-duration: 10ms

// Step 2: Implement Bulkhead Service
@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderProcessingService {
    
    @Bulkhead(name = "${user.tier:standard}-tier-bulkhead")
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "order-processing")
    public Order processOrder(OrderRequest request, String userTier) {
        log.info("Processing order for {} tier user", userTier);
        
        // Simulate processing
        try {
            Thread.sleep(2000); // Simulate work
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        }
        
        return Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .status(OrderStatus.PROCESSING)
            .build();
    }
}

// Step 3: Tenant-based Bulkhead Manager
@Component
public class TenantBulkheadManager {
    
    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Bulkhead> tenantBulkheads = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final TenantConfigService tenantConfigService;
    
    public TenantBulkheadManager(TenantConfigService tenantConfigService) {
        this.tenantConfigService = tenantConfigService;
    }
    
    public <T> T executeForTenant(String tenantId, Supplier<T> operation) {
        Bulkhead bulkhead = getOrCreateBulkhead(tenantId);
        
        try {
            return bulkhead.executeSupplier(operation);
        } catch (BulkheadFullException e) {
            log.warn("Bulkhead full for tenant: {}", tenantId);
            throw new TenantQuotaExceededException("Tenant " + tenantId + " has exceeded its concurrency limit");
        }
    }
    
    private Bulkhead getOrCreateBulkhead(String tenantId) {
        return tenantBulkheads.computeIfAbsent(tenantId, id -> {
            TenantConfig config = tenantConfigService.getTenantConfig(id);
            
            BulkheadConfig bulkheadConfig = BulkheadConfig.custom()
                .maxConcurrentCalls(config.getMaxConcurrentRequests())
                .maxWaitDuration(Duration.ofMillis(config.getMaxWaitTime()))
                .build();
            
            return Bulkhead.of(tenantId, bulkheadConfig);
        });
    }
}

// Step 4: ThreadPool Bulkhead for I/O Operations
@Configuration
public class ThreadPoolBulkheadConfig {
    
    @Bean
    @Qualifier("orderProcessingExecutor")
    public ExecutorService orderProcessingExecutor() {
        return new ThreadPoolExecutor(
            10,  // core pool size
            50,  // max pool size
            60L, // keep alive time
            TimeUnit.SECONDS,
            new LinkedBlockingQueue<>(100),
            new ThreadPoolExecutor.CallerRunsPolicy() // Backpressure
        );
    }
    
    @Bean
    public ThreadPoolBulkheadRegistry threadPoolBulkheadRegistry() {
        ThreadPoolBulkheadConfig config = ThreadPoolBulkheadConfig.custom()
            .maxThreadPoolSize(50)
            .coreThreadPoolSize(10)
            .queueCapacity(100)
            .keepAliveDuration(Duration.ofMinutes(1))
            .build();
        
        return ThreadPoolBulkheadRegistry.of(config);
    }
}

CQRS for Read/Write Separation
Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Step 1: Commands (Write Side)
@Value
@Builder
public class CreateOrderCommand {
    UUID customerId;
    List<OrderItemDto> items;
    Address shippingAddress;
    String notes;
}

@Value
@Builder
public class UpdateOrderStatusCommand {
    UUID orderId;
    OrderStatus newStatus;
}

// Step 2: Queries (Read Side)
@Value
@Builder
public class GetOrderQuery {
    UUID orderId;
}

@Value
@Builder
public class GetCustomerOrdersQuery {
    UUID customerId;
    int pageNumber;
    int pageSize;
    Optional<OrderStatus> status;
}

// Step 3: Command Handler
@Component
@Slf4j
public class CreateOrderCommandHandler {
    
    private final OrderWriteRepository orderWriteRepository;
    private final EventPublisher eventPublisher;
    private final TransactionTemplate transactionTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    public CreateOrderCommandHandler(OrderWriteRepository orderWriteRepository,
                                    EventPublisher eventPublisher,
                                    PlatformTransactionManager transactionManager) {
        this.orderWriteRepository = orderWriteRepository;
        this.eventPublisher = eventPublisher;
        this.transactionTemplate = new TransactionTemplate(transactionManager);
    }
    
    public Order handle(CreateOrderCommand command) {
        return transactionTemplate.execute(status -> {
            try {
                // Create order entity
                Order order = Order.builder()
                    .id(UUID.randomUUID())
                    .customerId(command.getCustomerId())
                    .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
                    .createdAt(Instant.now())
                    .items(command.getItems().stream()
                        .map(this::toOrderItem)
                        .collect(Collectors.toList()))
                    .totalAmount(calculateTotal(command.getItems()))
                    .shippingAddress(command.getShippingAddress())
                    .notes(command.getNotes())
                    .build();
                
                // Save to write database
                Order savedOrder = orderWriteRepository.save(order);
                
                // Publish event for read model updates
                eventPublisher.publish(OrderCreatedEvent.builder()
                    .orderId(savedOrder.getId())
                    .customerId(savedOrder.getCustomerId())
                    .totalAmount(savedOrder.getTotalAmount())
                    .createdAt(savedOrder.getCreatedAt())
                    .build());
                
                log.info("Order created: {}", savedOrder.getId());
                return savedOrder;
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.error("Failed to create order", e);
                status.setRollbackOnly();
                throw e;
            }
        });
    }
}

// Step 4: Query Handler
@Component
public class GetCustomerOrdersQueryHandler {
    
    private final OrderReadRepository orderReadRepository;
    
    public PagedResult<OrderSummary> handle(GetCustomerOrdersQuery query) {
        Pageable pageable = PageRequest.of(
            query.getPageNumber() - 1,
            query.getPageSize(),
            Sort.by("createdAt").descending()
        );
        
        Page<OrderSummary> page = orderReadRepository.findByCustomerId(
            query.getCustomerId(),
            query.getStatus().orElse(null),
            pageable
        );
        
        return PagedResult.<OrderSummary>builder()
            .items(page.getContent())
            .totalCount(page.getTotalElements())
            .pageNumber(query.getPageNumber())
            .pageSize(query.getPageSize())
            .build();
    }
}

// Step 5: Read Model (Denormalized for Query Performance)
@Entity
@Table(name = "order_read_model")
@Data
@NoArgsConstructor
@AllArgsConstructor
public class OrderReadModel {
    
    @Id
    private UUID orderId;
    
    private UUID customerId;
    private String customerName;
    private String customerEmail;
    
    @Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)
    private OrderStatus status;
    
    private BigDecimal totalAmount;
    private Instant createdAt;
    private Instant shippedAt;
    private Instant deliveredAt;
    
    @OneToMany(cascade = CascadeType.ALL)
    private List<OrderItemReadModel> items;
    
    @Embedded
    private Address shippingAddress;
}

// Step 6: Event Handler to Update Read Model
@Component
@Slf4j
@EventListener
public class OrderCreatedEventHandler {
    
    private final OrderReadRepository orderReadRepository;
    private final CustomerServiceClient customerServiceClient;
    
    @Async
    @TransactionalEventListener(phase = TransactionPhase.AFTER_COMMIT)
    public void handleOrderCreated(OrderCreatedEvent event) {
        log.info("Updating read model for order: {}", event.getOrderId());
        
        try {
            // Fetch customer details
            CustomerInfo customer = customerServiceClient.getCustomer(event.getCustomerId());
            
            // Create denormalized read model
            OrderReadModel readModel = new OrderReadModel();
            readModel.setOrderId(event.getOrderId());
            readModel.setCustomerId(event.getCustomerId());
            readModel.setCustomerName(customer.getName());
            readModel.setCustomerEmail(customer.getEmail());
            readModel.setStatus(OrderStatus.PENDING);
            readModel.setTotalAmount(event.getTotalAmount());
            readModel.setCreatedAt(event.getCreatedAt());
            
            orderReadRepository.save(readModel);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to update read model for order: {}", event.getOrderId(), e);
            // Schedule retry or move to dead letter queue
        }
    }
}
Saga Pattern for Distributed Transactions
Step-by-Step Implementation with Spring State Machine:

Step 1: Add Dependencies

xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.statemachine</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-statemachine-starter</artifactId>
    <version>3.2.1</version>
</dependency>
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.kafka</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-kafka</artifactId>
</dependency>
Step 2: Define Saga States and Events

java
public enum OrderSagaState {
    START,
    RESERVING_INVENTORY,
    INVENTORY_RESERVED,
    PROCESSING_PAYMENT,
    PAYMENT_PROCESSED,
    ORDER_CONFIRMED,
    COMPLETED,
    CANCELLED,
    ROLLBACK_INVENTORY,
    ROLLBACK_PAYMENT
}

public enum OrderSagaEvent {
    START_SAGA,
    INVENTORY_RESERVED,
    INVENTORY_RESERVATION_FAILED,
    PAYMENT_PROCESSED,
    PAYMENT_FAILED,
    CONFIRM_ORDER,
    CANCEL_ORDER,
    ROLLBACK_COMPLETED
}
Step 3: Create Saga State Machine

java
@Configuration
@EnableStateMachine
public class OrderSagaStateMachineConfig extends StateMachineConfigurerAdapter<OrderSagaState, OrderSagaEvent> {
    
    @Override
    public void configure(StateMachineStateConfigurer<OrderSagaState, OrderSagaEvent> states) throws Exception {
        states
            .withStates()
            .initial(OrderSagaState.START)
            .state(OrderSagaState.RESERVING_INVENTORY)
            .state(OrderSagaState.INVENTORY_RESERVED)
            .state(OrderSagaState.PROCESSING_PAYMENT)
            .state(OrderSagaState.PAYMENT_PROCESSED)
            .state(OrderSagaState.ORDER_CONFIRMED)
            .end(OrderSagaState.COMPLETED)
            .end(OrderSagaState.CANCELLED)
            .and()
            .withStates()
                .parent(OrderSagaState.RESERVING_INVENTORY)
                .initial(OrderSagaState.ROLLBACK_INVENTORY)
                .end(OrderSagaState.CANCELLED)
            .and()
            .withStates()
                .parent(OrderSagaState.PROCESSING_PAYMENT)
                .initial(OrderSagaState.ROLLBACK_PAYMENT)
                .end(OrderSagaState.CANCELLED);
    }
    
    @Override
    public void configure(StateMachineTransitionConfigurer<OrderSagaState, OrderSagaEvent> transitions) throws Exception {
        transitions
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.START)
                .target(OrderSagaState.RESERVING_INVENTORY)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.START_SAGA)
                .action(reserveInventoryAction())
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.RESERVING_INVENTORY)
                .target(OrderSagaState.INVENTORY_RESERVED)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.INVENTORY_RESERVED)
                .action(processPaymentAction())
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.RESERVING_INVENTORY)
                .target(OrderSagaState.CANCELLED)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.INVENTORY_RESERVATION_FAILED)
                .action(handleFailureAction())
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.INVENTORY_RESERVED)
                .target(OrderSagaState.PROCESSING_PAYMENT)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.START_SAGA)
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.PROCESSING_PAYMENT)
                .target(OrderSagaState.PAYMENT_PROCESSED)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.PAYMENT_PROCESSED)
                .action(confirmOrderAction())
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.PROCESSING_PAYMENT)
                .target(OrderSagaState.ROLLBACK_INVENTORY)
                .event(OrderSagaEvent.PAYMENT_FAILED)
                .action(rollbackInventoryAction())
            .and()
            .withExternal()
                .source(OrderSagaState.PAYMENT_PROCESSED)
Can you please also explain each topics with definition?
I'll provide a comprehensive explanation with definitions for each topic in the Microservices Production Readiness Checklist, along with Java/Spring Boot examples.

1. Service Design
Single Responsibility per Service
Definition: Each microservice should have one clear, focused purpose and only one reason to change. This follows the Single Responsibility Principle (SRP) from SOLID, ensuring services remain small, maintainable, and independently deployable.

Why it matters: When services have multiple responsibilities, changes in one area can affect others, making the system brittle and harder to maintain. It also makes it difficult to scale services independently.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// BAD EXAMPLE - Multiple responsibilities in one service
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/ecommerce")
public class EcommerceController {
    
    // This service handles everything - violations of SRP
    private final UserRepository userRepository;
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final PaymentRepository paymentRepository;
    
    @PostMapping("/users/register")
    public ResponseEntity<UserDto> registerUser(@RequestBody UserDto user) {
        // User management logic
        return ResponseEntity.ok(userRepository.save(user));
    }
    
    @PostMapping("/orders/create")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderDto> createOrder(@RequestBody OrderDto order) {
        // Order management logic
        return ResponseEntity.ok(orderRepository.save(order));
    }
    
    @PostMapping("/products/add")
    public ResponseEntity<ProductDto> addProduct(@RequestBody ProductDto product) {
        // Product management logic
        return ResponseEntity.ok(productRepository.save(product));
    }
}

// GOOD EXAMPLE - Separate services with single responsibility

// Service 1: User Service - ONLY handles user management
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/users")
@Slf4j
public class UserService {
    
    private final UserRepository userRepository;
    private final UserValidator userValidator;
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    
    public UserService(UserRepository userRepository, 
                      UserValidator userValidator,
                      KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate) {
        this.userRepository = userRepository;
        this.userValidator = userValidator;
        this.kafkaTemplate = kafkaTemplate;
    }
    
    @PostMapping("/register")
    public ResponseEntity<UserDto> registerUser(@Valid @RequestBody UserRegistrationDto userDto) {
        // Validate user data
        ValidationResult validation = userValidator.validate(userDto);
        if (!validation.isValid()) {
            return ResponseEntity.badRequest().body(validation.getErrors());
        }
        
        // Create user
        User user = User.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .email(userDto.getEmail())
            .name(userDto.getName())
            .createdAt(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        User savedUser = userRepository.save(user);
        
        // Publish event - other services can react without direct coupling
        kafkaTemplate.send("user-created", UserCreatedEvent.builder()
            .userId(savedUser.getId())
            .email(savedUser.getEmail())
            .timestamp(Instant.now())
            .build());
        
        log.info("User registered successfully: {}", savedUser.getId());
        return ResponseEntity.ok(UserDto.fromEntity(savedUser));
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{userId}")
    public ResponseEntity<UserDto> getUser(@PathVariable UUID userId) {
        return userRepository.findById(userId)
            .map(UserDto::fromEntity)
            .map(ResponseEntity::ok)
            .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());
    }
}

// Service 2: Order Service - ONLY handles order management
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderService {
    
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final OrderValidator orderValidator;
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    
    @PostMapping
    public ResponseEntity<OrderDto> createOrder(@Valid @RequestBody CreateOrderDto orderDto) {
        // Validate order
        ValidationResult validation = orderValidator.validate(orderDto);
        if (!validation.isValid()) {
            return ResponseEntity.badRequest().body(validation.getErrors());
        }
        
        // Create order
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .customerId(orderDto.getCustomerId())
            .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
            .totalAmount(calculateTotal(orderDto.getItems()))
            .items(orderDto.getItems())
            .createdAt(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        Order savedOrder = orderRepository.save(order);
        
        // Publish event - other services react asynchronously
        kafkaTemplate.send("order-created", OrderCreatedEvent.builder()
            .orderId(savedOrder.getId())
            .customerId(savedOrder.getCustomerId())
            .totalAmount(savedOrder.getTotalAmount())
            .timestamp(Instant.now())
            .build());
        
        log.info("Order created successfully: {}", savedOrder.getId());
        return ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.CREATED).body(OrderDto.fromEntity(savedOrder));
    }
}
API-First Contract Design (OpenAPI 3.0)
Definition: Designing the API contract (what the API does, what data it expects, and what it returns) before writing any implementation code. This ensures that the API is well-thought-out, documented, and agreed upon by all stakeholders (frontend, backend, third-party consumers) before development begins.

Why it matters: API-first approach prevents integration issues, ensures backward compatibility, and allows teams to work in parallel. It also enables automated client generation, testing, and documentation.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

Step 1: Define OpenAPI Specification First

yaml
# openapi/order-service.yaml
openapi: 3.0.0
info:
  title: Order Service API
  version: 2.0.0
  description: |
    Manages customer orders with full lifecycle support.
    Features:
    - Create orders with idempotency support
    - Track order status in real-time
    - Cancel orders within 30 minutes
    - List orders with filtering and pagination
  contact:
    name: API Support
    email: support@example.com
  license:
    name: Apache 2.0
    url: http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0.html

servers:
  - url: https://api.example.com/v2
    description: Production server
  - url: https://staging-api.example.com/v2
    description: Staging server
  - url: http://localhost:8080
    description: Development server

tags:
  - name: orders
    description: Order management operations

paths:
  /orders:
    post:
      tags:
        - orders
      summary: Create a new order
      description: Creates a new order with idempotency support. The idempotency key ensures duplicate requests don't create multiple orders.
      operationId: createOrder
      parameters:
        - name: Idempotency-Key
          in: header
          required: true
          schema:
            type: string
            format: uuid
          description: Unique key to ensure idempotency. Key is valid for 24 hours.
      requestBody:
        required: true
        content:
          application/json:
            schema:
              $ref: '#/components/schemas/CreateOrderRequest'
            examples:
              standard:
                summary: Standard order
                value:
                  customerId: "123e4567-e89b-12d3-a456-426614174000"
                  items:
                    - productId: "123e4567-e89b-12d3-a456-426614174001"
                      quantity: 2
                  shippingAddress:
                    street: "123 Main St"
                    city: "New York"
                    country: "USA"
      responses:
        '201':
          description: Order created successfully
          headers:
            Location:
              schema:
                type: string
                format: uri
              description: URL of the created order
          content:
            application/json:
              schema:
                $ref: '#/components/schemas/OrderResponse'
        '400':
          description: Bad request - validation error
          content:
            application/json:
              schema:
                $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorResponse'
        '409':
          description: Duplicate order detected (idempotency key already used)
          content:
            application/json:
              schema:
                $ref: '#/components/schemas/ErrorResponse'
        '429':
          description: Too many requests - rate limit exceeded
          headers:
            X-RateLimit-Limit:
              schema:
                type: integer
            X-RateLimit-Remaining:
              schema:
                type: integer
            X-RateLimit-Reset:
              schema:
                type: integer

  /orders/{orderId}:
    get:
      tags:
        - orders
      summary: Get order by ID
      operationId: getOrder
      parameters:
        - name: orderId
          in: path
          required: true
          schema:
            type: string
            format: uuid
          description: The order ID
      responses:
        '200':
          description: Order found
          content:
            application/json:
              schema:
                $ref: '#/components/schemas/OrderResponse'
        '404':
          description: Order not found

components:
  schemas:
    CreateOrderRequest:
      type: object
      required:
        - customerId
        - items
        - shippingAddress
      properties:
        customerId:
          type: string
          format: uuid
          description: ID of the customer placing the order
        items:
          type: array
          minItems: 1
          maxItems: 50
          items:
            $ref: '#/components/schemas/OrderItem'
        shippingAddress:
          $ref: '#/components/schemas/Address'
        notes:
          type: string
          maxLength: 500
          description: Optional notes for the order
    
    OrderItem:
      type: object
      required:
        - productId
        - quantity
      properties:
        productId:
          type: string
          format: uuid
        quantity:
          type: integer
          minimum: 1
          maximum: 999
        unitPrice:
          type: number
          format: decimal
          readOnly: true
          description: Price at time of order (populated by server)
    
    OrderResponse:
      type: object
      properties:
        id:
          type: string
          format: uuid
        customerId:
          type: string
          format: uuid
        status:
          type: string
          enum:
            - pending
            - confirmed
            - processing
            - shipped
            - delivered
            - cancelled
        createdAt:
          type: string
          format: date-time
        updatedAt:
          type: string
          format: date-time
        totalAmount:
          type: number
          format: decimal
        items:
          type: array
          items:
            $ref: '#/components/schemas/OrderItem'
        shippingAddress:
          $ref: '#/components/schemas/Address'
        trackingNumber:
          type: string
          nullable: true
    
    Address:
      type: object
      properties:
        street:
          type: string
        city:
          type: string
        state:
          type: string
        country:
          type: string
        zipCode:
          type: string
    
    ErrorResponse:
      type: object
      properties:
        error:
          type: string
        code:
          type: string
        message:
          type: string
        requestId:
          type: string
          format: uuid
        timestamp:
          type: string
          format: date-time
Step 2: Implement the Contract with Spring Boot

java
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v2/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderController {
    
    private final OrderService orderService;
    
    @PostMapping
    @ResponseStatus(HttpStatus.CREATED)
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> createOrder(
            @Valid @RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request,
            @RequestHeader("Idempotency-Key") UUID idempotencyKey) {
        
        log.info("Creating order with idempotency key: {}", idempotencyKey);
        
        OrderResponse order = orderService.createOrder(request, idempotencyKey);
        
        return ResponseEntity
            .created(URI.create("/api/v2/orders/" + order.getId()))
            .body(order);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/{orderId}")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> getOrder(@PathVariable UUID orderId) {
        log.debug("Fetching order: {}", orderId);
        
        return orderService.getOrder(orderId)
            .map(ResponseEntity::ok)
            .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());
    }
}

// Request/Response DTOs
@Data
@Builder
@NoArgsConstructor
@AllArgsConstructor
public class CreateOrderRequest {
    @NotNull
    @UUID
    private UUID customerId;
    
    @NotEmpty
    @Size(max = 50)
    private List<@Valid OrderItem> items;
    
    @NotNull
    @Valid
    private Address shippingAddress;
    
    @Size(max = 500)
    private String notes;
}

@Data
@Builder
@NoArgsConstructor
@AllArgsConstructor
public class OrderResponse {
    private UUID id;
    private UUID customerId;
    private OrderStatus status;
    private Instant createdAt;
    private Instant updatedAt;
    private BigDecimal totalAmount;
    private List<OrderItem> items;
    private Address shippingAddress;
    private String trackingNumber;
}
Versioned APIs
Definition: Managing multiple versions of APIs simultaneously to ensure backward compatibility while allowing the API to evolve. This allows existing clients to continue using older versions while new clients can adopt newer versions.

Why it matters: Breaking changes are inevitable as APIs evolve. Versioning ensures that existing clients don't break when you introduce new features or modify existing behavior. It provides a smooth upgrade path and maintains trust with API consumers.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Option 1: URL Path Versioning (Most common)
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v1/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderControllerV1 {
    
    private final OrderService orderService;
    
    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderV1> getOrder(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // V1 returns limited fields for backward compatibility
        Order order = orderService.getOrder(id);
        
        OrderV1 response = OrderV1.builder()
            .id(order.getId())
            .total(order.getTotalAmount())  // Field name changed in V2
            .status(order.getStatus().name())
            .build();
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(response);
    }
}

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/v2/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderControllerV2 {
    
    private final OrderService orderService;
    
    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderV2> getOrder(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // V2 returns enhanced response
        Order order = orderService.getOrder(id);
        
        OrderV2 response = OrderV2.builder()
            .id(order.getId())
            .totalAmount(order.getTotalAmount())  // More descriptive field name
            .status(order.getStatus())
            .items(order.getItems())  // New field
            .createdAt(order.getCreatedAt())  // New field
            .customerInfo(getCustomerInfo(order.getCustomerId()))  // New nested object
            .build();
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(response);
    }
}

// Option 2: Content Negotiation (Header Versioning)
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    @GetMapping(value = "/{id}", produces = "application/vnd.company.order.v1+json")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderV1> getOrderV1(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // V1 implementation
        return ResponseEntity.ok(orderService.getOrderV1(id));
    }
    
    @GetMapping(value = "/{id}", produces = "application/vnd.company.order.v2+json")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderV2> getOrderV2(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // V2 implementation
        return ResponseEntity.ok(orderService.getOrderV2(id));
    }
}

// Option 3: Parameter Versioning (Query Parameter)
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/{id}")
    public ResponseEntity<?> getOrder(
            @PathVariable UUID id,
            @RequestParam(defaultValue = "1") int version) {
        
        if (version == 1) {
            return ResponseEntity.ok(orderService.getOrderV1(id));
        } else if (version == 2) {
            return ResponseEntity.ok(orderService.getOrderV2(id));
        } else {
            return ResponseEntity.badRequest().build();
        }
    }
}

// Versioned DTOs
@Data
@Builder
public class OrderV1 {
    private UUID id;
    private BigDecimal total;  // Old field name
    private String status;     // String vs enum
}

@Data
@Builder
public class OrderV2 {
    private UUID id;
    private BigDecimal totalAmount;  // Improved field name
    private OrderStatus status;      // Strongly typed enum
    private List<OrderItem> items;   // New field
    private Instant createdAt;       // New field
    private CustomerInfo customerInfo;  // New nested object
}

// Deprecation Strategy
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    @GetMapping(value = "/{id}", produces = "application/vnd.company.order.v1+json")
    @Deprecated
    @ApiOperation(value = "Get order (deprecated)", deprecated = true)
    @ApiResponses(value = {
        @ApiResponse(responseCode = "200", description = "Success"),
        @ApiResponse(responseCode = "410", description = "Version deprecated")
    })
    public ResponseEntity<OrderV1> getOrderV1(@PathVariable UUID id) {
        // Add deprecation warning in headers
        HttpHeaders headers = new HttpHeaders();
        headers.add("Warning", "299 - \"This API version is deprecated. Please use v2.\"");
        headers.add("Deprecation", "true");
        headers.add("Sunset", "Wed, 31 Dec 2025 23:59:59 GMT");
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok()
            .headers(headers)
            .body(orderService.getOrderV1(id));
    }
}
Idempotent Operations
Definition: An operation that can be performed multiple times without changing the result beyond the initial application. For POST operations (which typically create resources), idempotency ensures that duplicate requests don't create duplicate resources.

Why it matters: In distributed systems, network failures can cause clients to retry requests. Without idempotency, these retries could create duplicate records, process payments twice, or cause other data inconsistencies. Idempotency ensures reliability and data integrity.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class IdempotentOrderService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    public IdempotentOrderService(RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate,
                                  OrderRepository orderRepository,
                                  ObjectMapper objectMapper) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.orderRepository = orderRepository;
        this.objectMapper = objectMapper;
    }
    
    /**
     * Creates an order with idempotency guarantee
     * @param idempotencyKey Unique key provided by client
     * @param request The order request
     * @return OrderResponse with status
     */
    public OrderResponse createOrderWithIdempotency(String idempotencyKey, 
                                                   CreateOrderRequest request) {
        
        // Validate idempotency key format
        if (!isValidUUID(idempotencyKey)) {
            throw new InvalidIdempotencyKeyException("Idempotency key must be a valid UUID");
        }
        
        // Step 1: Check if already processed
        String cachedResult = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("idempotent:" + idempotencyKey);
        if (cachedResult != null) {
            log.info("Idempotent request {} already processed, returning cached result", 
                     idempotencyKey);
            return deserializeResponse(cachedResult);
        }
        
        // Step 2: Acquire distributed lock to prevent race conditions
        Boolean lockAcquired = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
            .setIfAbsent("lock:" + idempotencyKey, "locked", 
                         Duration.ofSeconds(30));
        
        if (Boolean.FALSE.equals(lockAcquired)) {
            log.warn("Could not acquire lock for idempotency key: {}", idempotencyKey);
            
            // Wait and retry to get the result
            return waitForResult(idempotencyKey);
        }
        
        try {
            // Step 3: Double-check after acquiring lock
            String doubleCheck = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
                .get("idempotent:" + idempotencyKey);
            if (doubleCheck != null) {
                return deserializeResponse(doubleCheck);
            }
            
            // Step 4: Check if order already exists with this idempotency key
            Optional<Order> existingOrder = orderRepository
                .findByIdempotencyKey(idempotencyKey);
            if (existingOrder.isPresent()) {
                OrderResponse response = OrderResponse.fromEntity(existingOrder.get());
                cacheResult(idempotencyKey, response);
                return response;
            }
            
            // Step 5: Execute the actual operation
            log.info("Processing idempotent request: {}", idempotencyKey);
            
            Order order = Order.builder()
                .id(UUID.randomUUID())
                .customerId(request.getCustomerId())
                .idempotencyKey(idempotencyKey)
                .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
                .items(request.getItems())
                .totalAmount(calculateTotal(request.getItems()))
                .createdAt(Instant.now())
                .build();
            
            // Save to database within a transaction
            Order savedOrder = orderRepository.save(order);
            
            // Publish event for downstream processing
            publishOrderCreatedEvent(savedOrder);
            
            // Step 6: Cache the result
            OrderResponse response = OrderResponse.fromEntity(savedOrder);
            cacheResult(idempotencyKey, response);
            
            log.info("Successfully processed idempotent request: {}", idempotencyKey);
            return response;
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to process idempotent request: {}", idempotencyKey, e);
            throw new OrderCreationException("Failed to create order", e);
        } finally {
            // Release lock
            redisTemplate.delete("lock:" + idempotencyKey);
        }
    }
    
    private void cacheResult(String idempotencyKey, OrderResponse response) {
        try {
            String serialized = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(response);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                "idempotent:" + idempotencyKey,
                serialized,
                Duration.ofHours(24)  // Cache for 24 hours
            );
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            log.error("Failed to cache idempotent result", e);
        }
    }
    
    private OrderResponse waitForResult(String idempotencyKey) {
        // Poll for up to 5 seconds to get the result
        for (int i = 0; i < 50; i++) {
            try {
                Thread.sleep(100);
                
                String cached = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
                    .get("idempotent:" + idempotencyKey);
                if (cached != null) {
                    return deserializeResponse(cached);
                }
            } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                break;
            }
        }
        
        throw new RequestProcessingException(
            "Request is being processed. Please check status later.", 
            HttpStatus.ACCEPTED);
    }
    
    private OrderResponse deserializeResponse(String json) {
        try {
            return objectMapper.readValue(json, OrderResponse.class);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to deserialize cached response", e);
        }
    }
}

// Using database for idempotency with optimistic locking
@Entity
@Table(name = "orders", uniqueConstraints = {
    @UniqueConstraint(name = "uk_idempotency_key", columnNames = "idempotency_key")
})
@Data
public class Order {
    @Id
    private UUID id;
    
    @Column(name = "idempotency_key", unique = true, nullable = false)
    private String idempotencyKey;
    
    private UUID customerId;
    
    @Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)
    private OrderStatus status;
    
    @Version
    private Long version;  // Optimistic locking
    
    // Other fields...
}

// Controller implementation
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
@Slf4j
public class OrderController {
    
    private final IdempotentOrderService orderService;
    
    @PostMapping
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> createOrder(
            @Valid @RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request,
            @RequestHeader(value = "Idempotency-Key", required = false) String idempotencyKey) {
        
        // Generate idempotency key if not provided
        if (idempotencyKey == null || idempotencyKey.isEmpty()) {
            idempotencyKey = UUID.randomUUID().toString();
            log.warn("Client didn't provide idempotency key, generated: {}", idempotencyKey);
        }
        
        OrderResponse response = orderService.createOrderWithIdempotency(
            idempotencyKey, 
            request
        );
        
        return ResponseEntity
            .status(HttpStatus.CREATED)
            .header("Idempotency-Key", idempotencyKey)
            .body(response);
    }
    
    @GetMapping("/idempotency/{key}")
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> checkIdempotentRequest(
            @PathVariable String key) {
        
        return orderService.findByIdempotencyKey(key)
            .map(OrderResponse::fromEntity)
            .map(ResponseEntity::ok)
            .orElse(ResponseEntity.notFound().build());
    }
}
Stateless Service Design
Definition: A stateless service does not store any client-specific data between requests. Each request contains all the information needed to process it, and the service doesn't rely on any stored session data. State is externalized to databases, caches, or other storage systems.

Why it matters: Stateless services are inherently scalable, resilient, and simpler to manage. They can be horizontally scaled (add more instances) without complex session replication. Any instance can handle any request, making load balancing trivial and enabling graceful failure recovery.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// BAD EXAMPLE - Stateful service
@Service
public class StatefulOrderService {
    
    // In-memory state - BAD for distributed systems
    private final Map<UUID, Order> inMemoryOrders = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private LocalDateTime lastProcessedTime;
    private int requestCount;  // Counter that won't be consistent across instances
    
    public Order createOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
            .build();
        
        // Storing state in memory - will be lost on restart
        inMemoryOrders.put(order.getId(), order);
        requestCount++;
        
        return order;
    }
    
    public Order getOrder(UUID id) {
        // State is only in this instance's memory
        return inMemoryOrders.get(id);
    }
}

// GOOD EXAMPLE - Stateless service
@Service
@Slf4j
public class StatelessOrderService {
    
    // No instance state - all state is externalized
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;  // External database
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;  // External cache
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;  // External messaging
    
    public Order createOrder(OrderRequest request, String authToken) {
        // All needed information is passed in or retrieved from external systems
        
        // Validate with external service
        CustomerInfo customer = getCustomerFromExternalService(authToken);
        
        // Create order
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .customerId(customer.getId())
            .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
            .requestId(getRequestIdFromContext())  // From thread-local, not instance state
            .createdAt(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        // Store in external database
        Order savedOrder = orderRepository.save(order);
        
        // Cache in external cache
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
            "order:" + savedOrder.getId(), 
            savedOrder, 
            Duration.ofMinutes(30)
        );
        
        // Publish to external messaging system
        kafkaTemplate.send("order-created", savedOrder);
        
        log.info("Order created: {}", savedOrder.getId());
        return savedOrder;
    }
    
    public Optional<Order> getOrder(UUID id) {
        // Retrieve from external cache first
        Order cached = (Order) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("order:" + id);
        if (cached != null) {
            return Optional.of(cached);
        }
        
        // Fallback to database
        return orderRepository.findById(id);
    }
    
    private CustomerInfo getCustomerFromExternalService(String authToken) {
        // Call external customer service via REST/gRPC
        // No state stored in this service
        return customerServiceClient.getCustomer(authToken);
    }
    
    private String getRequestIdFromContext() {
        // Get from MDC or request context
        return MDC.get("requestId");
    }
}

// Session handling in stateless service
@Configuration
public class StatelessSecurityConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public SecurityFilterChain filterChain(HttpSecurity http) throws Exception {
        http
            .sessionManagement(session -> session
                .sessionCreationPolicy(SessionCreationPolicy.STATELESS)  // No sessions
            )
            .authorizeHttpRequests(auth -> auth
                .anyRequest().authenticated()
            )
            .oauth2ResourceServer(oauth2 -> oauth2
                .jwt(jwt -> jwt
                    .jwtAuthenticationConverter(jwtAuthenticationConverter())
                )
            );
        
        return http.build();
    }
    
    @Bean
    public JwtDecoder jwtDecoder() {
        // JWT contains all session information - no server-side state
        return NimbusJwtDecoder.withJwkSetUri("https://auth-server/.well-known/jwks.json")
            .build();
    }
}

// Kubernetes deployment for stateless service - can scale horizontally
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: order-service
spec:
  replicas: 5  # Easy horizontal scaling
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: order-service
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: order-service
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: order-service
        image: order-service:latest
        env:
        - name: SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE
          value: "production"
        - name: DB_CONNECTION_STRING
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: db-secrets
              key: connection-string
        resources:
          requests:
            memory: "512Mi"
            cpu: "250m"
          limits:
            memory: "1Gi"
            cpu: "500m"
Graceful Degradation
Definition: The ability of a system to continue operating at a reduced level of functionality when some components fail or are unavailable, rather than completely failing. It prioritizes core functionality and provides fallback mechanisms.

Why it matters: In distributed systems, failures are inevitable. Graceful degradation ensures that users can still perform critical operations even when non-critical services are down, improving user experience and system reliability.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class OrderServiceWithGracefulDegradation {
    
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final PaymentServiceClient paymentClient;
    private final InventoryServiceClient inventoryClient;
    private final NotificationServiceClient notificationClient;
    private final CircuitBreakerFactory circuitBreakerFactory;
    
    @Autowired
    public OrderServiceWithGracefulDegradation(OrderRepository orderRepository,
                                               PaymentServiceClient paymentClient,
                                               InventoryServiceClient inventoryClient,
                                               NotificationServiceClient notificationClient,
                                               CircuitBreakerFactory circuitBreakerFactory) {
        this.orderRepository = orderRepository;
        this.paymentClient = paymentClient;
        this.inventoryClient = inventoryClient;
        this.notificationClient = notificationClient;
        this.circuitBreakerFactory = circuitBreakerFactory;
    }
    
    /**
     * Creates an order with graceful degradation
     * Core functionality always works, non-critical features degrade gracefully
     */
    @Transactional
    public OrderResponse createOrderWithGracefulDegradation(CreateOrderRequest request) {
        
        // Step 1: Core functionality - ALWAYS works
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .customerId(request.getCustomerId())
            .items(request.getItems())
            .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
            .createdAt(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        // Save order - this is the core operation
        Order savedOrder = orderRepository.save(order);
        log.info("Order created with ID: {}", savedOrder.getId());
        
        // Step 2: Attempt optional services with degradation
        // Each service call is isolated and won't affect the main flow
        
        // 2.1 Check inventory - degrade if unavailable
        try {
            InventoryCheckResult inventoryResult = inventoryClient.checkAvailability(
                savedOrder.getItems()
            );
            
            if (inventoryResult.isAvailable()) {
                savedOrder.setStatus(OrderStatus.CONFIRMED);
                savedOrder.setInventoryReserved(true);
                log.info("Inventory reserved for order: {}", savedOrder.getId());
            } else {
                savedOrder.setStatus(OrderStatus.PENDING_INVENTORY);
                savedOrder.setInventoryIssues(inventoryResult.getIssues());
                log.warn("Inventory issues for order: {}", savedOrder.getId());
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Inventory service unavailable, continuing with degraded state", e);
            savedOrder.setStatus(OrderStatus.PENDING_INVENTORY_CHECK);
            savedOrder.setInventoryCheckSkipped(true);
        }
        
        // 2.2 Process payment - degrade to manual if needed
        try {
            CircuitBreaker paymentBreaker = circuitBreakerFactory.create("payment-service");
            
            PaymentResult paymentResult = paymentBreaker.run(
                () -> paymentClient.processPayment(savedOrder),
                throwable -> {
                    // Fallback when payment service is down
                    log.warn("Payment service unavailable, marking for manual processing", 
                             throwable);
                    return PaymentResult.builder()
                        .status(PaymentStatus.PENDING_MANUAL)
                        .message("Payment processing temporarily unavailable. " +
                                "Order will be processed manually.")
                        .build();
                }
            );
            
            if (paymentResult.getStatus() == PaymentStatus.SUCCESS) {
                savedOrder.setPaymentStatus(PaymentStatus.COMPLETED);
                savedOrder.setPaymentId(paymentResult.getPaymentId());
            } else if (paymentResult.getStatus() == PaymentStatus.PENDING_MANUAL) {
                savedOrder.setPaymentStatus(PaymentStatus.PENDING_MANUAL);
                savedOrder.setRequiresManualIntervention(true);
            } else {
                savedOrder.setPaymentStatus(PaymentStatus.FAILED);
                savedOrder.setPaymentError(paymentResult.getErrorMessage());
            }
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Payment processing failed, marking for manual intervention", e);
            savedOrder.setPaymentStatus(PaymentStatus.PENDING_MANUAL);
            savedOrder.setRequiresManualIntervention(true);
            savedOrder.setPaymentError(e.getMessage());
        }
        
        // 2.3 Send notifications - degrade gracefully (non-critical)
        try {
            notificationClient.sendOrderConfirmation(savedOrder);
            log.info("Order confirmation sent for: {}", savedOrder.getId());
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.warn("Failed to send order confirmation, but order is saved", e);
            // Don't throw - notification is non-critical
            savedOrder.setNotificationSent(false);
            savedOrder.setNotificationError(e.getMessage());
        }
        
        // 2.4 Update analytics - fire and forget (best effort)
        CompletableFuture.runAsync(() -> {
            try {
                analyticsClient.recordOrder(savedOrder);
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.warn("Analytics recording failed, but order is processed", e);
            }
        });
        
        // Update order with final status
        savedOrder.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
        orderRepository.save(savedOrder);
        
        // Build response with degradation information
        return OrderResponse.builder()
            .id(savedOrder.getId())
            .status(savedOrder.getStatus())
            .totalAmount(savedOrder.getTotalAmount())
            .degradationInfo(collectDegradationInfo(savedOrder))
            .build();
    }
    
    /**
     * Get order with cached fallback if service is degraded
     */
    public OrderResponse getOrderWithDegradation(UUID orderId) {
        try {
            // Try to get fresh data
            return orderRepository.findById(orderId)
                .map(OrderResponse::fromEntity)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new OrderNotFoundException(orderId));
                
        } catch (DatabaseException e) {
            log.error("Database unavailable, using cached data", e);
            
            // Fallback to cache
            Order cachedOrder = getFromLocalCache(orderId);
            if (cachedOrder != null) {
                return OrderResponse.builder()
                    .id(cachedOrder.getId())
                    .status(cachedOrder.getStatus())
                    .totalAmount(cachedOrder.getTotalAmount())
                    .fromCache(true)
                    .cacheTimestamp(cachedOrder.getLastCachedAt())
                    .build();
            }
            
            // Ultimate fallback - return minimal information
            return OrderResponse.builder()
                .id(orderId)
                .status(OrderStatus.UNKNOWN)
                .errorMessage("System experiencing issues. Please check back later.")
                .degraded(true)
                .build();
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Health check that reflects degradation status
     */
    @GetMapping("/health/degradation")
    public ResponseEntity<HealthStatus> getHealthWithDegradation() {
        HealthStatus health = new HealthStatus();
        health.setOverall(HealthStatus.UP);
        
        // Check each dependency
        try {
            paymentClient.healthCheck();
            health.setPaymentService(HealthStatus.UP);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            health.setPaymentService(HealthStatus.DEGRADED);
            health.setDegradedFunctions(Arrays.asList(
                "Automatic payment processing disabled",
                "Orders will be processed manually"
            ));
        }
        
        try {
            inventoryClient.healthCheck();
            health.setInventoryService(HealthStatus.UP);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            health.setInventoryService(HealthStatus.DEGRADED);
            health.setDegradedFunctions(Arrays.asList(
                "Inventory checks disabled",
                "Orders accepted without real-time inventory verification"
            ));
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(health);
    }
}

// Controller showing degradation information
@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api/orders")
public class OrderController {
    
    @PostMapping
    public ResponseEntity<OrderResponse> createOrder(@Valid @RequestBody CreateOrderRequest request) {
        OrderResponse response = orderService.createOrderWithGracefulDegradation(request);
        
        // Add warnings in headers if degradation occurred
        HttpHeaders headers = new HttpHeaders();
        if (response.isDegraded()) {
            headers.add("Warning", "299 - \"Service degraded - some features unavailable\"");
            headers.add("X-Degraded-Features", response.getDegradedFeatures().toString());
        }
        
        return ResponseEntity
            .status(HttpStatus.CREATED)
            .headers(headers)
            .body(response);
    }
}
2. Resilience & Fault Tolerance
Circuit Breaker Pattern (Resilience4j)
Definition: A pattern that prevents a system from repeatedly trying to execute an operation that's likely to fail. It monitors failures and "trips" (opens) when failures reach a threshold, allowing the system to fail fast and recover gracefully. The circuit breaker has three states: CLOSED (normal operation), OPEN (failing fast), and HALF-OPEN (testing recovery).

Why it matters: Without circuit breakers, a failing downstream service can cause cascading failures, thread pool exhaustion, and system-wide degradation. Circuit breakers provide stability and prevent failures from propagating.

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Step 1: Add dependencies
// pom.xml
<dependency>
    <groupId>io.github.resilience4j</groupId>
    <artifactId>resilience4j-spring-boot2</artifactId>
    <version>2.1.0</version>
</dependency>
<dependency>
    <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
    <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-actuator</artifactId>
</dependency>

// Step 2: Configuration
@Configuration
public class CircuitBreakerConfiguration {
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerRegistry circuitBreakerRegistry() {
        CircuitBreakerConfig config = CircuitBreakerConfig.custom()
            // Sliding window to count failures
            .slidingWindowType(CircuitBreakerConfig.SlidingWindowType.COUNT_BASED)
            .slidingWindowSize(10)  // Consider last 10 calls
            .minimumNumberOfCalls(5)  // Need at least 5 calls to evaluate
            .permittedNumberOfCallsInHalfOpenState(3)  // Allow 3 test calls when half-open
            .waitDurationInOpenState(Duration.ofSeconds(30))  // Stay open for 30 seconds
            .failureRateThreshold(50)  // Trip when 50% of calls fail
            .recordExceptions(HttpServerErrorException.class, TimeoutException.class)
            .ignoreExceptions(BusinessException.class)  // Don't count business exceptions
            .build();
        
        return CircuitBreakerRegistry.of(config);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public CircuitBreakerAspect circuitBreakerAspect(CircuitBreakerRegistry registry) {
        return new CircuitBreakerAspect(registry);
    }
}

// Step 3: Implement circuit breaker in service
@Service
@Slf4j
public class PaymentServiceClient {
    
    private final RestTemplate restTemplate;
    private final CircuitBreaker circuitBreaker;
    private final MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    public PaymentServiceClient(RestTemplate restTemplate,
                               CircuitBreakerRegistry registry,
                               MeterRegistry meterRegistry) {
        this.restTemplate = restTemplate;
        this.circuitBreaker = registry.circuitBreaker("payment-service");
        this.meterRegistry = meterRegistry;
        
        // Add event listeners
        circuitBreaker.getEventPublisher()
            .onSuccess(event -> {
                log.info("Circuit breaker success: {}", event);
                meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.success", 
                    "service", "payment").increment();
            })
            .onError(event -> {
                log.warn("Circuit breaker error: {}", event);
                meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.error", 
                    "service", "payment").increment();
            })
            .onCallNotPermitted(event -> {
                log.warn("Circuit breaker is OPEN, call not permitted");
                meterRegistry.counter("circuitbreaker.rejected", 
                    "service", "payment").increment();
            })
            .onStateTransition(event -> {
                log.info("Circuit breaker state transition: {} -> {}", 
                    event.getStateTransition().getFromState(),
                    event.getStateTransition().getToState());
                
                meterRegistry.gauge("circuitbreaker.state", 
                    event.getStateTransition().getToState().ordinal());
            });
    }
    
    public PaymentResponse processPayment(PaymentRequest request) {
        // Execute with circuit breaker
        return circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
            log.info("Processing payment for order: {}", request.getOrderId());
            
            try {
                ResponseEntity<PaymentResponse> response = restTemplate.postForEntity(
                    "http://payment-service/api/v1/payments",
                    request,
                    PaymentResponse.class
                );
                
                return response.getBody();
            } catch (HttpServerErrorException e) {
                log.error("Payment service error: {}", e.getStatusCode());
                throw e;  // Will be counted as failure
            } catch (ResourceAccessException e) {
                log.error("Payment service unreachable", e);
                throw new RuntimeException("Payment service unavailable", e);
            }
        });
    }
    
    public PaymentResponse processPaymentWithFallback(PaymentRequest request) {
        try {
            return circuitBreaker.executeSupplier(() -> {
                return restTemplate.postForObject(
                    "http://payment-service/api/v1/payments",
                    request,
                    PaymentResponse.class
                );
            });
        } catch (CallNotPermittedException e) {
            // Circuit is OPEN - use fallback
            log.warn("Circuit breaker is open, using fallback");
            return PaymentResponse.fallback()
                .status(PaymentStatus.PENDING)
                .message("Payment service unavailable. Order will be processed manually.")
                .build();
        }
    }
}

// Step 4: Alternative approach with annotations
@Service
@Slf4j
public class InventoryServiceClient {
    
    @CircuitBreaker(name = "inventory-service", fallbackMethod = "checkAvailabilityFallback")
    @Retry(name = "inventory-service")
    @TimeLimiter(name = "inventory-service")
    public CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> checkAvailability(List<OrderItem> items) {
        // Simulate async call
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            return restTemplate.postForObject(
                "http://inventory-service/api/v1/check",
                items,
                InventoryResponse.class
            );
        });
    }
    
    public CompletableFuture<InventoryResponse> checkAvailabilityFallback(
            List<OrderItem> items, Exception ex) {
        
        log.warn("Inventory service unavailable, using fallback response", ex);
        
        // Fallback response - assume items are available
        return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(
            InventoryResponse.builder()
                .available(true)
                .message("Inventory check unavailable, assuming items are available")
                .fallbackUsed(true)
                .build()
        );
    }
}
