                gen.writeNull();
                return;
            }
            
            // Check if field has PII annotation
            // Implementation details...
        }
    }
}

// Usage in event class
@Data
@Builder
public class CustomerOrderEvent {
    private UUID customerId;
    
    @PII(value = MaskingType.EMAIL)
    private String email;  // Will be masked: joh***@ex****.com
    
    @PII(value = MaskingType.PARTIAL)
    private String phone;  // Will be masked: +1******890
    
    @PII(value = MaskingType.CREDIT_CARD)
    private String creditCard;  // Will be masked: ****-****-****-1234
    
    private Address address;  // Address might contain PII too
}

// Producer that automatically masks PII
@Service
@Slf4j
public class SecureEventProducer {
    
    private final PIIMaskingService maskingService;
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    
    public void publishCustomerEvent(CustomerOrderEvent event) {
        // Automatically mask PII before publishing
        CustomerOrderEvent maskedEvent = maskingService.maskPII(event);
        
        log.info("Publishing event with masked PII: {}", maskedEvent);
        kafkaTemplate.send("customer-events", maskedEvent);
    }
}

4. Producer Patterns

Transactional Outbox Pattern
Definition: A pattern that ensures atomicity between database updates and event publishing. Instead of publishing events directly, events are stored in an "outbox" table within the same database transaction, then a separate process publishes them, guaranteeing at-least-once delivery.

sql
-- Outbox table schema
CREATE TABLE outbox (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    aggregate_type VARCHAR(100),
    aggregate_id VARCHAR(100),
    event_type VARCHAR(100),
    payload JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW(),
    processed_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
    retry_count INT DEFAULT 0,
    last_error TEXT,
    version INT DEFAULT 1
);

CREATE INDEX idx_outbox_unprocessed ON outbox(processed_at) WHERE processed_at IS NULL;
CREATE INDEX idx_outbox_aggregate ON outbox(aggregate_type, aggregate_id);
java
@Entity
@Table(name = "outbox")
@Data
@NoArgsConstructor
@AllArgsConstructor
public class OutboxMessage {
    
    @Id
    private UUID id;
    
    @Column(name = "aggregate_type")
    private String aggregateType;
    
    @Column(name = "aggregate_id")
    private String aggregateId;
    
    @Column(name = "event_type")
    private String eventType;
    
    @Column(columnDefinition = "jsonb")
    private String payload;
    
    @Column(name = "created_at")
    private Instant createdAt;
    
    @Column(name = "processed_at")
    private Instant processedAt;
    
    @Column(name = "retry_count")
    private int retryCount;
    
    @Column(name = "last_error")
    private String lastError;
    
    @Version
    private int version;
}

// Repository
public interface OutboxRepository extends JpaRepository<OutboxMessage, UUID> {
    
    @Query("SELECT o FROM OutboxMessage o WHERE o.processedAt IS NULL " +
           "ORDER BY o.createdAt ASC")
    List<OutboxMessage> findUnprocessedMessages(Pageable pageable);
    
    @Modifying
    @Query("UPDATE OutboxMessage o SET o.processedAt = :processedAt, " +
           "o.retryCount = o.retryCount + 1 WHERE o.id = :id")
    int markAsProcessed(@Param("id") UUID id, @Param("processedAt") Instant processedAt);
}

// Service using transactional outbox
@Service
@Slf4j
@Transactional
public class OrderServiceWithOutbox {
    
    private final OrderRepository orderRepository;
    private final OutboxRepository outboxRepository;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    @Autowired
    public OrderServiceWithOutbox(OrderRepository orderRepository,
                                  OutboxRepository outboxRepository,
                                  ObjectMapper objectMapper) {
        this.orderRepository = orderRepository;
        this.outboxRepository = outboxRepository;
        this.objectMapper = objectMapper;
    }
    
    public Order createOrder(CreateOrderRequest request) {
        // 1. Create order in database
        Order order = Order.builder()
            .id(UUID.randomUUID())
            .customerId(request.getCustomerId())
            .status(OrderStatus.PENDING)
            .totalAmount(calculateTotal(request.getItems()))
            .createdAt(Instant.now())
            .build();
        
        orderRepository.save(order);
        
        // 2. Create outbox message in same transaction
        OrderCreatedEvent event = OrderCreatedEvent.builder()
            .eventId(UUID.randomUUID())
            .orderId(order.getId())
            .customerId(order.getCustomerId())
            .totalAmount(order.getTotalAmount())
            .createdAt(order.getCreatedAt())
            .build();
        
        OutboxMessage outbox = new OutboxMessage();
        outbox.setId(UUID.randomUUID());
        outbox.setAggregateType("Order");
        outbox.setAggregateId(order.getId().toString());
        outbox.setEventType("OrderCreated");
        outbox.setPayload(toJson(event));
        outbox.setCreatedAt(Instant.now());
        
        outboxRepository.save(outbox);
        
        log.info("Order created and outbox message stored: {}", order.getId());
        return order;
    }
    
    private String toJson(Object event) {
        try {
            return objectMapper.writeValueAsString(event);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to serialize event", e);
        }
    }
}

// Outbox publisher - runs as a background process
@Component
@Slf4j
public class OutboxPublisher {
    
    private final OutboxRepository outboxRepository;
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, String> kafkaTemplate;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    private final MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 1000)  // Run every second
    public void publishOutboxMessages() {
        Pageable pageable = PageRequest.of(0, 100);
        List<OutboxMessage> messages = outboxRepository.findUnprocessedMessages(pageable);
        
        for (OutboxMessage message : messages) {
            try {
                // Publish to Kafka
                kafkaTemplate.send(
                    message.getEventType(),
                    message.getAggregateId(),
                    message.getPayload()
                ).get(10, TimeUnit.SECONDS);  // Wait for acknowledgment
                
                // Mark as processed
                outboxRepository.markAsProcessed(message.getId(), Instant.now());
                
                // Record metrics
                meterRegistry.counter("outbox.published", 
                    "event.type", message.getEventType()).increment();
                
                log.debug("Published outbox message: {}", message.getId());
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.error("Failed to publish outbox message: {}", message.getId(), e);
                
                // Update error info
                message.setLastError(e.getMessage());
                outboxRepository.save(message);
                
                meterRegistry.counter("outbox.publish.failed",
                    "event.type", message.getEventType()).increment();
            }
        }
    }
    
    // Dead letter queue for failed messages
    @EventListener
    public void handleOutboxFailure(OutboxPublishFailureEvent event) {
        OutboxMessage message = event.getMessage();
        
        if (message.getRetryCount() >= 5) {
            log.error("Outbox message {} failed after {} retries, moving to DLQ",
                message.getId(), message.getRetryCount());
            
            // Move to dead letter queue
            moveToDeadLetterQueue(message);
            
            // Delete from outbox
            outboxRepository.delete(message);
        }
    }
}

Idempotency Key on Every Event

Definition: Each event contains a unique idempotency key (usually event ID) that allows consumers to detect and ignore duplicate events, ensuring exactly-once processing semantics.

java
@Configuration
@Slf4j
public class IdempotentEventProducer {
    
    @Bean
    public KafkaTemplate<String, Object> idempotentKafkaTemplate() {
        Map<String, Object> config = new HashMap<>();
        config.put(ProducerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "kafka:9092");
        
        // Enable idempotent producer
        config.put(ProducerConfig.ENABLE_IDEMPOTENCE_CONFIG, true);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.ACKS_CONFIG, "all");
        config.put(ProducerConfig.RETRIES_CONFIG, Integer.MAX_VALUE);
        config.put(ProducerConfig.MAX_IN_FLIGHT_REQUESTS_PER_CONNECTION, 5);
        
        return new KafkaTemplate<>(new DefaultKafkaProducerFactory<>(config));
    }
}

// Event with idempotency key
@Data
@Builder
@NoArgsConstructor
@AllArgsConstructor
public class IdempotentEvent {
    private UUID eventId;           // Idempotency key
    private String eventType;
    private String correlationId;
    private Instant timestamp;
    private Object payload;
    
    // For deduplication
    private String getDeduplicationKey() {
        return eventType + ":" + eventId.toString();
    }
}

// Producer that ensures idempotent events
@Service
@Slf4j
public class IdempotentEventProducerService {
    
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    private final RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    
    public void publishIdempotentEvent(String topic, IdempotentEvent event) {
        // Check if we've already published this event
        String dedupKey = event.getDeduplicationKey();
        Boolean alreadyPublished = redisTemplate.hasKey("published:" + dedupKey);
        
        if (Boolean.TRUE.equals(alreadyPublished)) {
            log.info("Event {} already published, skipping", dedupKey);
            return;
        }
        
        // Publish with idempotent producer
        kafkaTemplate.send(topic, event.getEventId().toString(), event)
            .addCallback(
                result -> {
                    // Mark as published
                    redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                        "published:" + dedupKey,
                        "true",
                        Duration.ofDays(7)
                    );
                    log.info("Published event {} to partition {}", 
                        dedupKey, result.getRecordMetadata().partition());
                },
                ex -> log.error("Failed to publish event", ex)
            );
    }
}

// Consumer that handles idempotency
@Component
@Slf4j
public class IdempotentEventConsumer {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Boolean> processedEvents = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    
    @KafkaListener(topics = "order-events", groupId = "order-processor")
    public void consumeEvent(ConsumerRecord<String, IdempotentEvent> record) {
        IdempotentEvent event = record.value();
        String dedupKey = event.getDeduplicationKey();
        
        // Check if already processed (use Redis for distributed state)
        Boolean processed = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
            .setIfAbsent("processed:" + dedupKey, "true", Duration.ofDays(30));
        
        if (Boolean.FALSE.equals(processed)) {
            log.info("Event {} already processed, skipping", dedupKey);
            return;
        }
        
        try {
            // Process the event
            processEvent(event);
            
            // Mark as processed (already done by Redis SETNX)
            log.info("Processed event {}", dedupKey);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to process event {}, removing deduplication lock", dedupKey, e);
            // Remove lock so it can be retried
            redisTemplate.delete("processed:" + dedupKey);
            throw e;
        }
    }
}

5. Error Handling

Dead Letter Queue per Consumer
Definition: A dedicated queue (topic) where messages that cannot be processed successfully are stored for later analysis, replay, or manual intervention.

java
@Configuration
@Slf4j
public class DeadLetterQueueConfig {
    
    @Bean
    public ConcurrentKafkaListenerContainerFactory<String, Object> 
            deadLetterQueueContainerFactory() {
        
        ConcurrentKafkaListenerContainerFactory<String, Object> factory = 
            new ConcurrentKafkaListenerContainerFactory<>();
        
        Map<String, Object> config = new HashMap<>();
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "kafka:9092");
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.GROUP_ID_CONFIG, "order-consumer-group");
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.KEY_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, StringDeserializer.class);
        config.put(ConsumerConfig.VALUE_DESERIALIZER_CLASS_CONFIG, JsonDeserializer.class);
        
        // Configure error handler with DLQ
        factory.setCommonErrorHandler(new DefaultErrorHandler(
            new DeadLetterPublishingRecoverer(kafkaTemplate()),
            new FixedBackOff(1000L, 3)  // Retry 3 times with 1 second delay
        ));
        
        factory.setConsumerFactory(new DefaultKafkaConsumerFactory<>(config));
        
        return factory;
    }
    
    private KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate() {
        return new KafkaTemplate<>(new DefaultKafkaProducerFactory<>(
            Map.of(ProducerConfig.BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS_CONFIG, "kafka:9092")
        ));
    }
}

// Custom DLQ handler
@Component
@Slf4j
public class DeadLetterQueueHandler {
    
    private final KafkaTemplate<String, Object> kafkaTemplate;
    private final MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    public void sendToDeadLetterQueue(ConsumerRecord<?, ?> record, Exception exception, 
                                      String consumerGroup) {
        
        // Create DLQ message with error details
        DeadLetterMessage dlqMessage = DeadLetterMessage.builder()
            .originalTopic(record.topic())
            .originalPartition(record.partition())
            .originalOffset(record.offset())
            .consumerGroup(consumerGroup)
            .payload(record.value())
            .errorType(exception.getClass().getSimpleName())
            .errorMessage(exception.getMessage())
            .timestamp(Instant.now())
            .stackTrace(getStackTrace(exception))
            .retryCount(extractRetryCount(record))
            .headers(record.headers().toArray())
            .build();
        
        // Send to DLQ topic
        String dlqTopic = record.topic() + ".dlq";
        kafkaTemplate.send(dlqTopic, dlqMessage);
        
        // Record metrics
        meterRegistry.counter("dead.letter.queue.messages",
            "topic", record.topic(),
            "error", exception.getClass().getSimpleName()
        ).increment();
        
        log.error("Message sent to DLQ: topic={}, offset={}, error={}",
            record.topic(), record.offset(), exception.getMessage());
    }
    
    // Scheduled job to replay DLQ messages
    @Scheduled(cron = "0 0 * * * *")  // Every hour
    public void replayDeadLetterQueue() {
        // Fetch DLQ messages
        List<DeadLetterMessage> dlqMessages = fetchFromDLQ();
        
        for (DeadLetterMessage message : dlqMessages) {
            // Check if it's time to replay
            if (shouldReplay(message)) {
                try {
                    // Republish to original topic
                    kafkaTemplate.send(message.getOriginalTopic(), message.getPayload());
                    log.info("Replayed message from DLQ: {}", message.getOriginalOffset());
                    
                    // Mark as replayed
                    markAsReplayed(message);
                    
                } catch (Exception e) {
                    log.error("Failed to replay message from DLQ", e);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

// DLQ Alerting
@Component
@Slf4j
public class DLQAlertingService {
    
    private final MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    private final AlertingClient alertingClient;
    
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 60000)  // Check every minute
    public void checkDLQAlerts() {
        String[] topics = {"order-events.dlq", "payment-events.dlq", "inventory-events.dlq"};
        
        for (String topic : topics) {
            long dlqCount = getDLQCount(topic);
            
            if (dlqCount > 100) {
                // Send alert
                Alert alert = Alert.builder()
                    .severity(AlertSeverity.HIGH)
                    .title("High DLQ messages")
                    .message(String.format("DLQ topic %s has %d messages", topic, dlqCount))
                    .timestamp(Instant.now())
                    .build();
                
                alertingClient.sendAlert(alert);
                log.warn("High DLQ count detected for topic {}: {}", topic, dlqCount);
            }
            
            // Record metric
            meterRegistry.gauge("dead.letter.queue.size", 
                Tags.of("topic", topic), dlqCount);
        }
    }
}
=================================================
Overview of Caching Strategies
Caching is a critical performance optimization technique that stores frequently accessed data in a fast storage layer (like Redis) to reduce database load and improve response times. Each strategy has different trade-offs between consistency, performance, and complexity.

1. Read-Through Cache
Definition: In read-through caching, the application treats the cache as the primary data source. When reading data, the cache itself is responsible for loading missing data from the database. The application never directly accesses the database for reads.

How it works:

Application requests data from cache

If data exists (cache hit), cache returns it

If data doesn't exist (cache miss), cache loads it from database, stores it, then returns it

Pros:

Simple application logic (just call cache)

Centralized cache loading logic

Consistent data access pattern

Cons:

Cache becomes a dependency (if cache fails, reads fail)

First request for any key is slower (cache miss penalty)

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
// Step 1: Add dependencies in pom.xml
<dependencies>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-data-redis</artifactId>
    </dependency>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
        <artifactId>spring-boot-starter-data-jpa</artifactId>
    </dependency>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>redis.clients</groupId>
        <artifactId>jedis</artifactId>
    </dependency>
    <dependency>
        <groupId>org.postgresql</groupId>
        <artifactId>postgresql</artifactId>
    </dependency>
</dependencies>

// Step 2: Configure Redis
@Configuration
@EnableCaching
public class RedisConfig {
    
    @Value("${spring.redis.host}")
    private String redisHost;
    
    @Value("${spring.redis.port}")
    private int redisPort;
    
    @Bean
    public RedisConnectionFactory redisConnectionFactory() {
        RedisStandaloneConfiguration config = new RedisStandaloneConfiguration(redisHost, redisPort);
        return new LettuceConnectionFactory(config);
    }
    
    @Bean
    public RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate() {
        RedisTemplate<String, Object> template = new RedisTemplate<>();
        template.setConnectionFactory(redisConnectionFactory());
        
        // Configure serializers for proper JSON handling
        Jackson2JsonRedisSerializer<Object> jackson2JsonRedisSerializer = 
            new Jackson2JsonRedisSerializer<>(Object.class);
        
        ObjectMapper om = new ObjectMapper();
        om.setVisibility(PropertyAccessor.ALL, JsonAutoDetect.Visibility.ANY);
        om.activateDefaultTyping(LazyJackson2DefaultTypingNonFinalFactory.get());
        jackson2JsonRedisSerializer.setObjectMapper(om);
        
        template.setKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setValueSerializer(jackson2JsonRedisSerializer);
        template.setHashKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setHashValueSerializer(jackson2JsonRedisSerializer);
        
        template.afterPropertiesSet();
        return template;
    }
    
    @Bean
    public CacheManager cacheManager(RedisConnectionFactory redisConnectionFactory) {
        RedisCacheConfiguration cacheConfig = RedisCacheConfiguration.defaultCacheConfig()
            .entryTtl(Duration.ofMinutes(10))  // Time-to-live for cache entries
            .disableCachingNullValues()
            .serializeValuesWith(RedisSerializationContext.SerializationPair
                .fromSerializer(new GenericJackson2JsonRedisSerializer()));
        
        return RedisCacheManager.builder(redisConnectionFactory)
            .cacheDefaults(cacheConfig)
            .build();
    }
}

// Step 3: Implement Read-Through Cache with Repository Pattern
@Service
@Slf4j
public class ReadThroughCacheService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
    
    @Autowired
    public ReadThroughCacheService(RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate,
                                   ProductRepository productRepository,
                                   ObjectMapper objectMapper) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.objectMapper = objectMapper;
    }
    
    /**
     * Read-Through Implementation
     * Application always reads from cache, cache loads from DB if needed
     */
    public Product getProductWithReadThrough(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        // Step 1: Try to get from cache
        log.info("Attempting to read product {} from cache", productId);
        Product cachedProduct = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        
        if (cachedProduct != null) {
            // Cache hit - return cached data
            log.info("Cache HIT for product {}", productId);
            return cachedProduct;
        }
        
        // Step 2: Cache miss - load from database
        log.info("Cache MISS for product {}, loading from database", productId);
        Product dbProduct = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found: " + productId));
        
        // Step 3: Store in cache for future requests
        log.info("Storing product {} in cache", productId);
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, dbProduct, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
        
        return dbProduct;
    }
    
    /**
     * Read-Through with expiration and null handling
     */
    public Product getProductWithTTL(Long productId, Duration ttl) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        // Try cache with configurable TTL
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        
        if (product != null) {
            return product;
        }
        
        // Cache miss - handle carefully
        try {
            product = productRepository.findById(productId)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
            
            // Store with specified TTL
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, ttl);
            log.info("Cached product {} with TTL {} minutes", productId, ttl.toMinutes());
            
        } catch (ProductNotFoundException e) {
            // Cache the fact that product doesn't exist to prevent repeated DB hits
            // This is called "null caching" or "negative caching"
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, "NULL", Duration.ofMinutes(5));
            log.warn("Product {} not found, caching negative result", productId);
            throw e;
        }
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Bulk read-through for multiple products
     */
    public List<Product> getProductsBulkWithReadThrough(List<Long> productIds) {
        List<Product> products = new ArrayList<>();
        List<Long> missingIds = new ArrayList<>();
        
        // Step 1: Try to get all from cache using pipeline for efficiency
        List<Object> cachedProducts = redisTemplate.executePipelined(
            (RedisCallback<Object>) connection -> {
                for (Long id : productIds) {
                    String key = ("product:" + id).getBytes();
                    connection.get(key);
                }
                return null;
            }
        );
        
        // Step 2: Separate found and missing
        for (int i = 0; i < productIds.size(); i++) {
            Object cached = cachedProducts.get(i);
            if (cached != null && !cached.toString().equals("NULL")) {
                products.add((Product) cached);
                log.debug("Cache HIT for product {}", productIds.get(i));
            } else {
                missingIds.add(productIds.get(i));
                log.debug("Cache MISS for product {}", productIds.get(i));
            }
        }
        
        // Step 3: Load missing from database
        if (!missingIds.isEmpty()) {
            List<Product> dbProducts = productRepository.findAllById(missingIds);
            
            // Step 4: Store in cache and add to results
            for (Product product : dbProducts) {
                String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
                products.add(product);
                log.info("Cached product {} from bulk load", product.getId());
            }
            
            // Handle missing products that weren't in DB
            missingIds.removeIf(id -> dbProducts.stream()
                .anyMatch(p -> p.getId().equals(id)));
            
            for (Long missingId : missingIds) {
                // Cache nulls for missing products
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set("product:" + missingId, "NULL", 
                    Duration.ofMinutes(5));
                log.warn("Product {} not found, caching null", missingId);
            }
        }
        
        return products;
    }
}

2. Write-Through Cache

Definition: In write-through caching, every write operation goes through the cache. The cache is updated first, then immediately writes through to the database. This ensures cache and database remain consistent.

How it works:

Application writes data to cache

Cache immediately writes to database

Both operations complete before returning to application

Pros:

Strong consistency between cache and database

No stale data issues

Cache always has latest data

Cons:

Higher write latency (both operations must complete)

If cache fails, writes fail

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class WriteThroughCacheService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final TransactionTemplate transactionTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    public WriteThroughCacheService(RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate,
                                   ProductRepository productRepository,
                                   PlatformTransactionManager transactionManager) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.transactionTemplate = new TransactionTemplate(transactionManager);
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-Through Implementation
     * Write to cache first, then to database
     */
    @Transactional
    public Product createProductWithWriteThrough(Product product) {
        log.info("Creating product with write-through: {}", product.getName());
        
        // Step 1: Validate product
        if (product.getName() == null || product.getName().trim().isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Product name is required");
        }
        
        // Step 2: Write to cache first
        String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
        log.debug("Writing to cache first: {}", cacheKey);
        
        try {
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
            log.info("Cache updated for product {}", product.getId());
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to write to cache", e);
            // If cache fails, we might want to fail the operation or proceed?
            throw new CacheWriteException("Failed to write to cache", e);
        }
        
        // Step 3: Write to database immediately
        try {
            Product savedProduct = productRepository.save(product);
            log.info("Database updated for product {}", product.getId());
            return savedProduct;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            // If database fails, we need to rollback cache
            log.error("Database write failed, rolling back cache", e);
            redisTemplate.delete(cacheKey);
            throw new DatabaseWriteException("Failed to write to database", e);
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Update with write-through
     */
    @Transactional
    public Product updateProductWithWriteThrough(Long productId, Product updatedProduct) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        log.info("Updating product {} with write-through", productId);
        
        // Step 1: Check if product exists
        Product existingProduct = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found: " + productId));
        
        // Step 2: Merge updates
        existingProduct.setName(updatedProduct.getName());
        existingProduct.setPrice(updatedProduct.getPrice());
        existingProduct.setStock(updatedProduct.getStock());
        existingProduct.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
        
        // Step 3: Update cache first
        log.debug("Updating cache first for product {}", productId);
        try {
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, existingProduct, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
            log.info("Cache updated for product {}", productId);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to update cache", e);
            throw new CacheWriteException("Failed to update cache", e);
        }
        
        // Step 4: Update database
        try {
            Product savedProduct = productRepository.save(existingProduct);
            log.info("Database updated for product {}", productId);
            return savedProduct;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            // If database fails, revert cache to old value
            log.error("Database update failed, reverting cache", e);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, existingProduct, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
            throw new DatabaseWriteException("Failed to update database", e);
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Batch write-through for multiple products
     */
    @Transactional
    public List<Product> bulkCreateWithWriteThrough(List<Product> products) {
        List<Product> savedProducts = new ArrayList<>();
        List<String> cacheKeys = new ArrayList<>();
        
        try {
            // Step 1: Generate IDs and prepare cache keys
            for (Product product : products) {
                if (product.getId() == null) {
                    product.setId(IdGenerator.nextId());
                }
                String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
                cacheKeys.add(cacheKey);
            }
            
            // Step 2: Write all to cache (using pipeline for efficiency)
            log.info("Writing {} products to cache", products.size());
            redisTemplate.executePipelined((RedisCallback<Object>) connection -> {
                for (int i = 0; i < products.size(); i++) {
                    Product product = products.get(i);
                    String key = ("product:" + product.getId()).getBytes();
                    byte[] value = serializeProduct(product);
                    connection.setEx(key, Duration.ofMinutes(30).getSeconds(), value);
                }
                return null;
            });
            log.info("Cache updated for all products");
            
            // Step 3: Write all to database
            savedProducts = productRepository.saveAll(products);
            log.info("Database updated for all products");
            
            return savedProducts;
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            // Step 4: Rollback cache on failure
            log.error("Batch write-through failed, rolling back cache", e);
            for (String cacheKey : cacheKeys) {
                redisTemplate.delete(cacheKey);
            }
            throw new BulkWriteException("Batch write-through failed", e);
        }
    }
    
    private byte[] serializeProduct(Product product) {
        try {
            ObjectMapper mapper = new ObjectMapper();
            return mapper.writeValueAsBytes(product);
        } catch (JsonProcessingException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to serialize product", e);
        }
    }
}

3. Cache-Aside (Lazy Loading)

Definition: Cache-aside is the most common caching strategy. The application is responsible for managing the cache. It first checks the cache, and if the data isn't there (cache miss), it loads from the database and stores it in the cache for future requests.

How it works:

Application checks cache for data

If found (cache hit), return data

If not found (cache miss), load from database, store in cache, then return

Pros:

Only requested data is cached (efficient memory usage)

Simple to implement

Cache failure doesn't affect reads (can fallback to database)

Cons:

Cache misses result in higher latency

Data can become stale if not invalidated properly

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class CacheAsideService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final CacheMetricsService metricsService;
    
    @Autowired
    public CacheAsideService(RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate,
                            ProductRepository productRepository,
                            CacheMetricsService metricsService) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.metricsService = metricsService;
    }
    
    /**
     * Cache-Aside Implementation with metrics
     */
    public Product getProductWithCacheAside(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        long startTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        
        // Step 1: Check cache first
        log.debug("Cache-Aside: Checking cache for product {}", productId);
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        
        if (product != null) {
            // Cache hit - return immediately
            long latency = System.currentTimeMillis() - startTime;
            metricsService.recordCacheHit("product", latency);
            log.info("Cache HIT for product {} (latency: {}ms)", productId, latency);
            return product;
        }
        
        // Step 2: Cache miss - load from database
        log.info("Cache MISS for product {}, loading from database", productId);
        long dbStartTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        
        product = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found: " + productId));
        
        long dbLatency = System.currentTimeMillis() - dbStartTime;
        metricsService.recordDatabaseQuery("product", dbLatency);
        
        // Step 3: Store in cache for future requests
        log.debug("Storing product {} in cache", productId);
        try {
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
            metricsService.recordCacheWrite("product", productId);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to store product in cache", e);
            // Cache failure shouldn't break the application
        }
        
        long totalLatency = System.currentTimeMillis() - startTime;
        metricsService.recordCacheMiss("product", totalLatency);
        log.info("Loaded product {} from DB (DB: {}ms, Total: {}ms)", 
            productId, dbLatency, totalLatency);
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Cache-Aside with distributed lock to prevent cache stampede
     * (Multiple requests for the same missing key overwhelming the database)
     */
    public Product getProductWithCacheAsideAndLock(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        String lockKey = "lock:product:" + productId;
        
        // Step 1: Check cache first
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        if (product != null) {
            return product;
        }
        
        // Step 2: Try to acquire lock (only one thread will load from DB)
        Boolean lockAcquired = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
            .setIfAbsent(lockKey, "locked", Duration.ofSeconds(10));
        
        if (Boolean.TRUE.equals(lockAcquired)) {
            try {
                // Double-check cache after acquiring lock
                product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
                if (product != null) {
                    return product;
                }
                
                // Load from database (only one thread does this)
                log.info("Lock acquired, loading product {} from database", productId);
                product = productRepository.findById(productId)
                    .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
                
                // Store in cache
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
                
                return product;
                
            } finally {
                // Release lock
                redisTemplate.delete(lockKey);
            }
        } else {
            // Step 3: Wait for the loading thread to finish
            log.debug("Lock not acquired, waiting for product {} to be cached", productId);
            
            int maxRetries = 10;
            int retryCount = 0;
            
            while (retryCount < maxRetries) {
                try {
                    Thread.sleep(100); // Wait 100ms
                    product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
                    if (product != null) {
                        log.info("Product {} loaded by another thread", productId);
                        return product;
                    }
                } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                    break;
                }
                retryCount++;
            }
            
            // Timeout - fallback to direct DB load
            log.warn("Timeout waiting for cache, loading product {} directly", productId);
            return productRepository.findById(productId)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Cache invalidation on update (Cache-Aside pattern)
     */
    @Transactional
    public Product updateProductWithCacheAside(Long productId, Product updatedProduct) {
        // Step 1: Update database first
        Product existingProduct = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        
        existingProduct.setName(updatedProduct.getName());
        existingProduct.setPrice(updatedProduct.getPrice());
        existingProduct.setStock(updatedProduct.getStock());
        existingProduct.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
        
        Product savedProduct = productRepository.save(existingProduct);
        log.info("Database updated for product {}", productId);
        
        // Step 2: Invalidate cache (delete, don't update)
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        redisTemplate.delete(cacheKey);
        log.info("Cache invalidated for product {}", productId);
        
        // Note: We don't update cache directly. Next read will load fresh data
        // This is the cache-aside pattern - write-through would update cache directly
        
        return savedProduct;
    }
    
    /**
     * Cache aside with Redis transactions for consistency
     */
    public Product updateProductWithAtomicOperation(Long productId, Product updatedProduct) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        // Use Redis transaction to ensure consistency
        SessionCallback<Object> sessionCallback = new SessionCallback<Object>() {
            @Override
            public Object execute(RedisOperations operations) throws DataAccessException {
                operations.watch(cacheKey);  // Watch the key for changes
                
                operations.multi();  // Begin transaction
                
                // Delete from cache
                operations.delete(cacheKey);
                
                // Execute
                List<Object> results = operations.exec();
                return results;
            }
        };
        
        try {
            // Update database in a transaction
            Product savedProduct = transactionTemplate.execute(status -> {
                Product existing = productRepository.findById(productId)
                    .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
                
                existing.setName(updatedProduct.getName());
                existing.setPrice(updatedProduct.getPrice());
                existing.setStock(updatedProduct.getStock());
                
                return productRepository.save(existing);
            });
            
            // Invalidate cache
            redisTemplate.execute(sessionCallback);
            log.info("Cache invalidated for product {}", productId);
            
            return savedProduct;
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            log.error("Failed to update product", e);
            throw new RuntimeException("Update failed", e);
        }
    }
}

4. Write-Around Cache

Definition: Write-around caching is a hybrid strategy where data is written directly to the database without being cached. Only read operations populate the cache. This is useful for data that is written frequently but read infrequently.

How it works:

Writes go directly to database (cache is bypassed)

Reads first check cache, then database if needed

Only read data is cached

Pros:

Cache doesn't get polluted with write-heavy data

Efficient memory usage for read-heavy workloads

Redundant writes are avoided

Cons:

First read after write will be a cache miss

Data inconsistency if reads happen before cache is populated

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class WriteAroundCacheService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final BloomFilter<String> bloomFilter;  // Optional: prevent cache misses for non-existent keys
    
    @Autowired
    public WriteAroundCacheService(RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate,
                                   ProductRepository productRepository) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.bloomFilter = new BloomFilterImpl<>(10000, 0.01);  // 10k expected insertions, 1% false positive rate
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-Around Implementation
     * Write directly to database without caching
     */
    @Transactional
    public Product createProductWithWriteAround(Product product) {
        log.info("Creating product with write-around: {}", product.getName());
        
        // Step 1: Validate
        if (product.getPrice() == null || product.getPrice().compareTo(BigDecimal.ZERO) <= 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Price must be greater than zero");
        }
        
        // Step 2: Write directly to database (cache bypassed)
        Product savedProduct = productRepository.save(product);
        log.info("Product {} saved to database (cache bypassed)", savedProduct.getId());
        
        // Step 3: Update bloom filter for future reads
        String bloomKey = "product:" + savedProduct.getId();
        bloomFilter.add(bloomKey);
        
        // Note: No cache write happens here
        // Cache will be populated only on first read
        
        return savedProduct;
    }
    
    /**
     * Read with write-around - only read operations populate cache
     */
    public Product getProductWithWriteAround(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        // Step 1: Check bloom filter first (optional optimization)
        // If bloom filter says product doesn't exist, skip cache and DB check
        if (!bloomFilter.mightContain(cacheKey)) {
            log.debug("Bloom filter indicates product {} doesn't exist", productId);
            throw new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found: " + productId);
        }
        
        // Step 2: Check cache (only for reads)
        log.debug("Checking cache for product {}", productId);
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        
        if (product != null) {
            log.info("Cache HIT for product {}", productId);
            return product;
        }
        
        // Step 3: Cache miss - load from database
        log.info("Cache MISS for product {}, loading from database", productId);
        product = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found: " + productId));
        
        // Step 4: Store in cache (only now does cache get populated)
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
        log.info("Product {} cached after first read", productId);
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Update with write-around - update database directly, invalidate cache
     */
    @Transactional
    public Product updateProductWithWriteAround(Long productId, Product updatedProduct) {
        log.info("Updating product {} with write-around", productId);
        
        // Step 1: Update database directly
        Product existingProduct = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        
        existingProduct.setName(updatedProduct.getName());
        existingProduct.setPrice(updatedProduct.getPrice());
        existingProduct.setStock(updatedProduct.getStock());
        existingProduct.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
        
        Product savedProduct = productRepository.save(existingProduct);
        log.info("Database updated for product {}", productId);
        
        // Step 2: Invalidate cache if it exists
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        Boolean deleted = redisTemplate.delete(cacheKey);
        if (Boolean.TRUE.equals(deleted)) {
            log.info("Cache invalidated for product {}", productId);
        }
        
        // Note: We don't write to cache on update
        // Next read will populate cache with fresh data
        
        return savedProduct;
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-around with selective caching for frequently accessed items
     */
    public Product getProductWithSmartCaching(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        String accessCountKey = "access_count:product:" + productId;
        
        // Step 1: Check cache
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        if (product != null) {
            // Increment access count
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().increment(accessCountKey);
            return product;
        }
        
        // Step 2: Load from database
        product = productRepository.findById(productId)
            .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
        
        // Step 3: Check access count to decide if we should cache
        String countStr = (String) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(accessCountKey);
        long accessCount = countStr != null ? Long.parseLong(countStr) : 0;
        
        // Only cache frequently accessed items (more than 5 times)
        if (accessCount > 5) {
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofMinutes(60));
            log.info("Product {} is frequently accessed ({} times), caching", 
                productId, accessCount);
        } else {
            log.debug("Product {} accessed {} times, not caching yet", 
                productId, accessCount);
        }
        
        // Increment access count for next time
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().increment(accessCountKey);
        redisTemplate.expire(accessCountKey, Duration.ofHours(24));
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-around with TTL optimization for hot data
     */
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 60000)  // Run every minute
    public void optimizeCacheTTL() {
        // Analyze access patterns and adjust TTL for hot items
        Set<String> keys = redisTemplate.keys("access_count:product:*");
        
        if (keys != null) {
            for (String accessKey : keys) {
                String countStr = (String) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(accessKey);
                if (countStr != null) {
                    long accessCount = Long.parseLong(countStr);
                    String productId = accessKey.split(":")[2];
                    String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
                    
                    // Adjust TTL based on access frequency
                    if (accessCount > 100) {
                        // Very hot data - longer TTL
                        redisTemplate.expire(cacheKey, Duration.ofHours(4));
                        log.debug("Product {} is very hot, TTL increased to 4 hours", productId);
                    } else if (accessCount > 20) {
                        // Warm data - standard TTL
                        redisTemplate.expire(cacheKey, Duration.ofHours(1));
                    }
                    
                    // Reset access count periodically
                    redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(accessKey, "0", Duration.ofHours(24));
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

5. Write-Back (Write-Behind) Cache

Definition: Write-back caching writes data to the cache first and immediately returns success to the application. The data is then asynchronously written to the database at a later time. This provides the lowest write latency but with potential data loss if the cache fails before writing to the database.

How it works:

Application writes to cache

Cache acknowledges write immediately

Data is asynchronously flushed to database in batches

Reads always check cache first

Pros:

Lowest write latency

Can batch writes for better database performance

Handles write spikes gracefully

Cons:

Risk of data loss if cache fails

Eventual consistency (reads may not see latest data)

Complex recovery mechanisms needed

Step-by-Step Implementation:

java
@Service
@Slf4j
public class WriteBackCacheService {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    private final ProductRepository productRepository;
    private final BlockingQueue<WriteBackOperation> writeQueue;
    private final ScheduledExecutorService executorService;
    private final AtomicLong pendingWrites = new AtomicLong(0);
    private final MeterRegistry meterRegistry;
    
    @Autowired
    public WriteBackCacheService(RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate,
                                ProductRepository productRepository,
                                MeterRegistry meterRegistry) {
        this.redisTemplate = redisTemplate;
        this.productRepository = productRepository;
        this.meterRegistry = meterRegistry;
        this.writeQueue = new LinkedBlockingQueue<>(10000);  // Buffer up to 10k writes
        this.executorService = Executors.newScheduledThreadPool(5);
        
        // Start the write-back worker
        startWriteBackWorker();
        
        // Start periodic flush
        executorService.scheduleAtFixedRate(this::flushWrites, 5, 5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-Back Implementation
     * Write to cache immediately, queue for async DB write
     */
    public Product createProductWithWriteBack(Product product) {
        log.info("Creating product with write-back: {}", product.getName());
        
        // Step 1: Generate ID if not present
        if (product.getId() == null) {
            product.setId(IdGenerator.nextId());
        }
        
        // Step 2: Write to cache immediately
        String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
        product.setCreatedAt(Instant.now());
        product.setStatus(ProductStatus.PENDING_SYNC);  // Mark as pending sync
        
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofHours(24));
        log.info("Product {} written to cache (pending DB sync)", product.getId());
        
        // Step 3: Queue for async database write
        WriteBackOperation operation = WriteBackOperation.builder()
            .operationType(OperationType.CREATE)
            .product(product)
            .timestamp(Instant.now())
            .retryCount(0)
            .build();
        
        boolean queued = writeQueue.offer(operation);
        if (!queued) {
            log.error("Write queue full, product {} will be synced later", product.getId());
            // In production, you might want to fallback to synchronous write
            // or use a different strategy
        }
        
        pendingWrites.incrementAndGet();
        meterRegistry.counter("writeback.queue.size").increment();
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Read with write-back - must check both cache and ensure consistency
     */
    public Product getProductWithWriteBack(Long productId) {
        String cacheKey = "product:" + productId;
        
        // Step 1: Check cache first (always)
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
        
        if (product != null) {
            log.debug("Cache HIT for product {}", productId);
            
            // Check if data is pending sync
            if (product.getStatus() == ProductStatus.PENDING_SYNC) {
                log.debug("Product {} is pending sync, using cached version", productId);
                // Still return cached data, but maybe add a warning
            }
            
            return product;
        }
        
        // Step 2: Cache miss - check database
        log.info("Cache MISS for product {}, checking database", productId);
        product = productRepository.findById(productId).orElse(null);
        
        if (product != null) {
            // Found in DB, update cache
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, product, Duration.ofHours(24));
            log.info("Product {} loaded from DB and cached", productId);
        }
        
        return product;
    }
    
    /**
     * Write-back worker that processes the queue
     */
    private void startWriteBackWorker() {
        executorService.submit(() -> {
            while (!Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()) {
                try {
                    // Batch process writes for efficiency
                    List<WriteBackOperation> batch = new ArrayList<>();
                    
                    // Drain up to 100 operations or wait for 1 second
                    WriteBackOperation first = writeQueue.poll(1, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
                    if (first != null) {
                        batch.add(first);
                        writeQueue.drainTo(batch, 99);  // Get up to 100 total
                    }
                    
                    if (!batch.isEmpty()) {
                        processBatch(batch);
                    }
                    
                } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                    break;
                } catch (Exception e) {
                    log.error("Error in write-back worker", e);
                }
            }
        });
    }
    
    /**
     * Process a batch of write-back operations
     */
    private void processBatch(List<WriteBackOperation> batch) {
        log.info("Processing write-back batch of {} operations", batch.size());
        
        // Group by operation type for efficiency
        Map<OperationType, List<WriteBackOperation>> grouped = batch.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(WriteBackOperation::getOperationType));
        
        for (Map.Entry<OperationType, List<WriteBackOperation>> entry : grouped.entrySet()) {
            try {
                switch (entry.getKey()) {
                    case CREATE:
                        batchCreateProducts(entry.getValue());
                        break;
                    case UPDATE:
                        batchUpdateProducts(entry.getValue());
                        break;
                    case DELETE:
                        batchDeleteProducts(entry.getValue());
                        break;
                }
                
                // Record success metrics
                meterRegistry.counter("writeback.batch.success", 
                    "size", String.valueOf(batch.size())).increment();
                
                pendingWrites.addAndGet(-batch.size());
                meterRegistry.gauge("writeback.pending.writes", pendingWrites);
                
            } catch (Exception e) {
                log.error("Failed to process batch", e);
                // Re-queue failed operations with retry count
                for (WriteBackOperation op : entry.getValue()) {
                    if (op.getRetryCount() < 5) {
                        op.setRetryCount(op.getRetryCount() + 1);
                        op.setLastError(e.getMessage());
                        writeQueue.offer(op);
                        log.warn("Re-queued operation for product {} (retry {}/5)", 
                            op.getProduct().getId(), op.getRetryCount());
                    } else {
                        // Max retries exceeded - move to dead letter queue
                        moveToDeadLetterQueue(op);
                        log.error("Operation for product {} failed after 5 retries", 
                            op.getProduct().getId());
                    }
                }
                
                meterRegistry.counter("writeback.batch.failed", 
                    "error", e.getClass().getSimpleName()).increment();
            }
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Batch create products in database
     */
    private void batchCreateProducts(List<WriteBackOperation> operations) {
        List<Product> products = operations.stream()
            .map(WriteBackOperation::getProduct)
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
        
        // Batch insert
        productRepository.saveAll(products);
        
        // Update cache to reflect synced status
        for (Product product : products) {
            String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
            Product cachedProduct = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
            if (cachedProduct != null) {
                cachedProduct.setStatus(ProductStatus.SYNCED);
                cachedProduct.setSyncedAt(Instant.now());
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, cachedProduct, Duration.ofHours(24));
            }
        }
        
        log.info("Batch created {} products", products.size());
    }
    
    /**
     * Batch update products with optimistic locking
     */
    private void batchUpdateProducts(List<WriteBackOperation> operations) {
        // Group by product ID to handle updates
        for (WriteBackOperation op : operations) {
            Product product = op.getProduct();
            
            // Use optimistic locking to handle concurrent updates
            int retries = 3;
            while (retries > 0) {
                try {
                    Product existing = productRepository.findById(product.getId())
                        .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Product not found"));
                    
                    // Apply updates
                    existing.setName(product.getName());
                    existing.setPrice(product.getPrice());
                    existing.setStock(product.getStock());
                    existing.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
                    
                    productRepository.save(existing);
                    
                    // Update cache
                    String cacheKey = "product:" + product.getId();
                    Product cached = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(cacheKey);
                    if (cached != null) {
                        cached.setStatus(ProductStatus.SYNCED);
                        cached.setSyncedAt(Instant.now());
                        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(cacheKey, cached, Duration.ofHours(24));
                    }
                    
                    break;
                    
                } catch (OptimisticLockingException e) {
                    retries--;
                    if (retries == 0) {
                        throw e;
                    }
                    log.warn("Optimistic lock conflict for product {}, retrying", product.getId());
                }
            }
        }
        
        log.info("Batch updated {} products", operations.size());
    }
    
    /**
     * Scheduled flush to ensure all writes eventually reach database
     */
    private void flushWrites() {
        int queueSize = writeQueue.size();
        if (queueSize > 0) {
            log.info("Periodic flush: {} pending writes in queue", queueSize);
            // The worker thread will process them
        }
        
        // Log metrics
        meterRegistry.gauge("writeback.queue.size", queueSize);
        meterRegistry.gauge("writeback.pending.writes", pendingWrites.get());
    }
    
    /**
     * Graceful shutdown - ensure all writes are flushed
     */
    @PreDestroy
    public void shutdown() {
        log.info("Shutting down write-back service, flushing remaining writes...");
        
        // Stop accepting new writes
        executorService.shutdown();
        
        // Flush remaining queue
        int remaining = writeQueue.size();
        if (remaining > 0) {
            List<WriteBackOperation> remainingOps = new ArrayList<>();
            writeQueue.drainTo(remainingOps);
            
            log.info("Flushing {} remaining operations", remainingOps.size());
            processBatch(remainingOps);
        }
        
        try {
            if (!executorService.awaitTermination(30, TimeUnit.SECONDS)) {
                executorService.shutdownNow();
            }
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            executorService.shutdownNow();
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        }
        
        log.info("Write-back service shutdown complete");
    }
    
    @Data
    @Builder
    private static class WriteBackOperation {
        private OperationType operationType;
        private Product product;
        private Instant timestamp;
        private int retryCount;
        private String lastError;
    }
    
    private enum OperationType {
        CREATE, UPDATE, DELETE
    }
    
    private enum ProductStatus {
        PENDING_SYNC, SYNCED
    }
}

Comparison Table of Caching Strategies
Strategy	Write Latency	Read Latency	Consistency	Complexity	Use Case
Read-Through	N/A	High (cache miss)	Strong	Medium	Read-heavy workloads
Write-Through	High	Low	Strong	Medium	Consistency-critical systems
Cache-Aside	Medium	Medium	Eventual	Low	Most common general purpose
Write-Around	Low	High (first read)	Eventual	Medium	Write-heavy, read-rarely data
Write-Back	Very Low	Low	Eventual	High	Extreme write performance needed

Best Practices for Redis Caching
java
@Component
@Slf4j
public class CacheBestPractices {
    
    private final RedisTemplate<String, Object> redisTemplate;
    
    /**
     * Practice 1: Use appropriate TTL based on data volatility
     */
    public void setWithTTL(String key, Object value, DataCategory category) {
        Duration ttl;
        switch (category) {
            case STATIC_DATA:
                ttl = Duration.ofDays(7);  // Reference data
                break;
            case FREQUENTLY_CHANGING:
                ttl = Duration.ofMinutes(5);  // Stock levels
                break;
            case SESSION_DATA:
                ttl = Duration.ofHours(1);  // User sessions
                break;
            default:
                ttl = Duration.ofMinutes(30);
        }
        
        redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(key, value, ttl);
        log.debug("Cached {} with TTL {}", key, ttl);
    }
    
    /**
     * Practice 2: Implement cache warming for critical data
     */
    @EventListener(ApplicationReadyEvent.class)
    public void warmCache() {
        log.info("Warming up cache...");
        
        // Load frequently accessed data
        List<Product> popularProducts = productRepository.findTop100ByOrderByAccessCountDesc();
        
        for (Product product : popularProducts) {
            String key = "product:" + product.getId();
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(key, product, Duration.ofHours(1));
        }
        
        log.info("Cache warmed with {} popular products", popularProducts.size());
    }
    
    /**
     * Practice 3: Use cache stampede prevention
     */
    public Product getProductWithStampedePrevention(Long id) {
        String key = "product:" + id;
        String lockKey = "lock:product:" + id;
        
        // Try to get from cache
        Product product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(key);
        if (product != null) {
            return product;
        }
        
        // Try to acquire lock
        Boolean locked = redisTemplate.opsForValue()
            .setIfAbsent(lockKey, "1", Duration.ofSeconds(5));
        
        if (Boolean.TRUE.equals(locked)) {
            try {
                // Load from database
                product = productRepository.findById(id)
                    .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Not found"));
                
                // Store in cache
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(key, product, Duration.ofMinutes(30));
                
                return product;
            } finally {
                redisTemplate.delete(lockKey);
            }
        } else {
            // Wait for the loading thread
            int retries = 0;
            while (retries < 50) {
                try {
                    Thread.sleep(100);
                    product = (Product) redisTemplate.opsForValue().get(key);
                    if (product != null) {
                        return product;
                    }
                } catch (InterruptedException e) {
                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
                    break;
                }
                retries++;
            }
            
            // Fallback to database
            return productRepository.findById(id)
                .orElseThrow(() -> new ProductNotFoundException("Not found"));
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Practice 4: Monitor cache hit rates
     */
    @Scheduled(fixedDelay = 60000)
    public void monitorCacheMetrics() {
        // Get Redis INFO stats
        Properties info = redisTemplate.getRequiredConnectionFactory()
            .getConnection().info("stats");
        
        long keyspaceHits = Long.parseLong(info.getProperty("keyspace_hits", "0"));
        long keyspaceMisses = Long.parseLong(info.getProperty("keyspace_misses", "0"));
        long totalOps = keyspaceHits + keyspaceMisses;
        
        double hitRate = totalOps > 0 ? (double) keyspaceHits / totalOps * 100 : 0;
        
        log.info("Cache hit rate: {:.2f}% (hits: {}, misses: {})", 
            hitRate, keyspaceHits, keyspaceMisses);
        
        // Record metrics
        meterRegistry.gauge("cache.hit.rate", hitRate);
        meterRegistry.counter("cache.hits", keyspaceHits);
        meterRegistry.counter("cache.misses", keyspaceMisses);
        
        // Alert if hit rate drops below threshold
        if (hitRate < 70 && totalOps > 1000) {
            log.warn("Low cache hit rate detected: {:.2f}%", hitRate);
        }
    }
}

Q:Design a thread-safe rate limiter using java steams api that allows: 
• Maximum 10 requests 
• Per 60 seconds

Complete Thread-Safe Rate Limiter Implementation

1. Basic Rate Limiter with Streams and Concurrent Collections
java
import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.concurrent.*;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;
import java.util.stream.Stream;

/**
 * Thread-safe Rate Limiter using Java Streams API
 * Allows maximum 10 requests per 60 seconds
 */
public class StreamBasedRateLimiter {
    
    private final ConcurrentLinkedDeque<Long> requestTimestamps;
    private final int maxRequests;
    private final long timeWindowMillis;
    private final AtomicLong requestCounter;
    private final Semaphore semaphore;
    
    public StreamBasedRateLimiter(int maxRequests, long timeWindowSeconds) {
        this.maxRequests = maxRequests;
        this.timeWindowMillis = timeWindowSeconds * 1000;
        this.requestTimestamps = new ConcurrentLinkedDeque<>();
        this.requestCounter = new AtomicLong(0);
        this.semaphore = new Semaphore(maxRequests);
    }
    
    /**
     * Thread-safe rate limit check using Stream API
     */
    public boolean tryAcquire() {
        long currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        long windowStart = currentTime - timeWindowMillis;
        
        // Clean up old timestamps using Stream API
        cleanUpOldTimestamps(windowStart);
        
        // Check if we can accept new request using Stream API
        long count = requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .count();
        
        boolean canAcquire = count < maxRequests;
        
        if (canAcquire) {
            requestTimestamps.add(currentTime);
            requestCounter.incrementAndGet();
        }
        
        return canAcquire;
    }
    
    /**
     * Clean up old timestamps using Stream API
     */
    private void cleanUpOldTimestamps(long windowStart) {
        // Use Stream to filter out old timestamps and collect remaining
        ConcurrentLinkedDeque<Long> filtered = requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .collect(Collectors.toCollection(ConcurrentLinkedDeque::new));
        
        // Clear and add all valid timestamps
        requestTimestamps.clear();
        requestTimestamps.addAll(filtered);
    }
    
    /**
     * Get current request count in the time window using Stream API
     */
    public long getCurrentRequestCount() {
        long currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        long windowStart = currentTime - timeWindowMillis;
        
        return requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .count();
    }
    
    /**
     * Get time until next available slot
     */
    public long getTimeUntilNextSlot() {
        long currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        long windowStart = currentTime - timeWindowMillis;
        
        // Get oldest request in current window using Stream
        return requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .min(Long::compareTo)
            .map(oldestTimestamp -> {
                long expirationTime = oldestTimestamp + timeWindowMillis;
                long waitTime = expirationTime - currentTime;
                return Math.max(0, waitTime);
            })
            .orElse(0L);
    }
    
    /**
     * Reset the rate limiter
     */
    public void reset() {
        requestTimestamps.clear();
        requestCounter.set(0);
    }
}

/**
 * Enhanced Rate Limiter with Metrics and Monitoring
 */
class EnhancedStreamRateLimiter {
    
    private final ConcurrentSkipListSet<Long> requestTimestamps;
    private final int maxRequests;
    private final long timeWindowMillis;
    private final AtomicLong totalRequests;
    private final AtomicLong rejectedRequests;
    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, Long> metrics;
    
    public EnhancedStreamRateLimiter(int maxRequests, long timeWindowSeconds) {
        this.maxRequests = maxRequests;
        this.timeWindowMillis = timeWindowSeconds * 1000;
        this.requestTimestamps = new ConcurrentSkipListSet<>();
        this.totalRequests = new AtomicLong(0);
        this.rejectedRequests = new AtomicLong(0);
        this.metrics = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
        
        // Start cleanup scheduler
        startCleanupScheduler();
    }
    
    /**
     * Try to acquire a permit with detailed metrics
     */
    public RateLimitResult tryAcquire(String clientId) {
        long currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        long windowStart = currentTime - timeWindowMillis;
        
        // Use parallel stream for better performance with large datasets
        long currentCount = requestTimestamps.parallelStream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .count();
        
        boolean allowed = currentCount < maxRequests;
        
        totalRequests.incrementAndGet();
        
        if (allowed) {
            requestTimestamps.add(currentTime);
            updateMetrics(clientId, true);
            return RateLimitResult.success(currentCount + 1, maxRequests, 0);
        } else {
            rejectedRequests.incrementAndGet();
            long retryAfter = calculateRetryAfter(windowStart);
            updateMetrics(clientId, false);
            return RateLimitResult.failure(currentCount, maxRequests, retryAfter);
        }
    }
    
    /**
     * Calculate retry-after time using Stream API
     */
    private long calculateRetryAfter(long windowStart) {
        return requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .sorted()
            .findFirst()
            .map(oldest -> oldest + timeWindowMillis - System.currentTimeMillis())
            .orElse(0L);
    }
    
    /**
     * Get sliding window statistics using Stream API
     */
    public WindowStatistics getWindowStatistics() {
        long currentTime = System.currentTimeMillis();
        long windowStart = currentTime - timeWindowMillis;
        
        // Collect all timestamps in the window
        List<Long> activeTimestamps = requestTimestamps.stream()
            .filter(timestamp -> timestamp > windowStart)
            .collect(Collectors.toList());
        
        if (activeTimestamps.isEmpty()) {
            return new WindowStatistics(0, 0, 0, 0);
        }
        
        // Calculate various statistics using Stream
        long count = activeTimestamps.size();
        long min = activeTimestamps.stream().min(Long::compareTo).orElse(0L);
        long max = activeTimestamps.stream().max(Long::compareTo).orElse(0L);
        double avg = activeTimestamps.stream()
            .mapToLong(Long::longValue)
            .average()
            .orElse(0.0);
        
        // Calculate request distribution over time segments
        Map<Integer, Long> distribution = activeTimestamps.stream()
            .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(
                timestamp -> (int) ((timestamp - windowStart) / (timeWindowMillis / 10)),
                Collectors.counting()
            ));
        
        return new WindowStatistics(count, min, max, avg, distribution);
    }
    
    private void updateMetrics(String clientId, boolean allowed) {
        String metricKey = allowed ? "allowed:" + clientId : "rejected:" + clientId;
        metrics.merge(metricKey, 1L, Long::sum);
    }
    
    private void startCleanupScheduler() {
        ScheduledExecutorService scheduler = Executors.newSingleThreadScheduledExecutor();
        scheduler.scheduleAtFixedRate(() -> {
            long windowStart = System.currentTimeMillis() - timeWindowMillis;
            // Clean up using Stream API
            requestTimestamps.removeIf(timestamp -> timestamp <= windowStart);
        }, 1, 1, TimeUnit.SECONDS);
    }
    
    public RateLimitMetrics getMetrics() {
        return new RateLimitMetrics(
            totalRequests.get(),
            rejectedRequests.get(),
            getCurrentRequestCount(),
            metrics
        );
    }
}
